commit 45a4c58697a7750809fbcc9bd2c35b43129fe1e0
Author: evnchn <user1@example.invalid>
Date:   Sun Oct 18 22:53:09 2026 +0800

    agent OS: tools/nc110-install-agentos.sh (versioned nc110-bm-agentos-<sha8> entry: GRUB-armed, any GRUB-side failure -> next_entry=0 one Linux boot; dedicated key ~/.nc110/agentos.key outside the repo; --set-default backs up grubenv; --print-entry for rehearsal); bmctl.py defaults to the agent-OS key; q35 GRUB 2.14 rehearsal: boots + answers with agent-OS key, test key rejected, missing kernel takes the fallback branch
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Install a kernel as an agent-OS version on the NC110 (run while Slot A Linux is up). Adds ONE new entry
# "nc110-bm-agentos-<sha8>" (never edits an existing one), copies /baremetal/k-agentos-<sha8>.elf and the agent-OS
# key module (~/.nc110/agentos.key -> /baremetal/nc110-agentos.key). Does NOT change the default: that is
#   tools/nc110-install-agentos.sh --set-default <sha8>     (backs up grubenv first)
# Entry behaviour: GRUB arms the TCO (nc110_tco_consts, lock_mode=accept), then multiboot + module + boot. ANY
# GRUB-side failure (arming refused, kernel or module missing) sets next_entry=0 and reboots: one boot of Slot A
# Linux instead of a loop, and leaves the breadcrumb nc110_fallback=<sha8> in grubenv (read it from Linux). Manual fallback any time: hold Shift through POST -> GRUB menu -> "Alpine NC110 ...".
set -u
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
S() { ssh -F /dev/null -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=5 -o StrictHostKeyChecking=no -o UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null \
  -o LogLevel=ERROR -i "$HOME/.ssh/id_ed25519_homelab" root@192.168.50.118 "$@"; }
SC() { scp -q -F /dev/null -o BatchMode=yes -o StrictHostKeyChecking=no -o UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null -o LogLevel=ERROR \
  -i "$HOME/.ssh/id_ed25519_homelab" "$@"; }
gen_entry() {  # $1 sha8 -> the exact menuentry text (also printed by --print-entry for the q35 rehearsal)
  local H=$1 E="nc110-bm-agentos-t2-$1" KN="k-agentos-$1.elf"   # t2: template 2 (fallback breadcrumb)
  ENTRY="menuentry \"$E\" {
    insmod ext2
    search --no-floppy --fs-uuid --set=root c0954bc7-86a0-440c-8401-2c3e95549743
    terminal_output console
    set gfxpayload=text
    insmod multiboot
    nc110_tco_consts
    tco_arm
    if [ \"\$wdt_ok\" = \"yes\" ]; then
      echo \"nc110-agentos $H: TCO armed by GRUB, loading /baremetal/$KN\"
      if multiboot /baremetal/$KN bmctl; then
        if module /baremetal/nc110-agentos.key; then
          boot
        fi
      fi
      echo \"nc110-agentos $H: kernel or key module failed to load\"
    else
      echo \"nc110-agentos $H: GRUB did NOT arm the TCO\"
    fi
    echo \"nc110-agentos: one boot of Slot A Linux instead (next_entry=0)\"
    set next_entry=0
    set nc110_fallback=$H
    save_env next_entry nc110_fallback
    sleep 5
    reboot
  }"
}
if [ "${1:-}" = "--print-entry" ]; then
  gen_entry "$(shasum -a 256 "$2" | cut -c1-8)"; printf '%s\n' "$ENTRY"; exit 0
fi
st=$(S 'hostname; awk "\$2==\"/\"{print \$1}" /proc/mounts') || { echo "Slot A not reachable"; exit 2; }
grep -qx nc110 <<<"$st" && grep -qx /dev/sda2 <<<"$st" || { echo "not on Slot A: STOP"; exit 2; }
T=$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ)
if [ "${1:-}" = "--set-default" ]; then
  E="nc110-bm-agentos-t2-$2"
  S "grep -q '^menuentry \"$E\"' /boot/grub/grub.cfg" || { echo "no entry $E"; exit 2; }
  S "cp -p /boot/grub/grubenv /boot/grub/grubenv.bak-agentos-$T && grub-editenv /boot/grub/grubenv set saved_entry='$E' && sync && grub-editenv /boot/grub/grubenv list"
  exit $?
fi
K=${1:?kernel path}; KEY=$HOME/.nc110/agentos.key
[ "$(wc -c <"$KEY" | tr -d " ")" = 72 ] || { echo "agent-OS key missing/bad"; exit 2; }
H=$(shasum -a 256 "$K" | cut -c1-8); E="nc110-bm-agentos-t2-$H"; KN=k-agentos-$H.elf
S "grep -q '^menuentry \"$E\"' /boot/grub/grub.cfg" && { echo "$E already installed"; exit 0; }
S "grep -q '^function nc110_tco_consts' /boot/grub/grub.cfg" || { echo "GRUB arming function missing"; exit 2; }
gen_entry "$H"
BK=/boot/grub/grub.cfg.bak-nc110bm-$T
S "cp -p /boot/grub/grub.cfg $BK" || exit 2
S "[ -e /boot/baremetal/$KN ]" || SC "$K" "root@192.168.50.118:/boot/baremetal/$KN" || exit 2
SC "$KEY" root@192.168.50.118:/boot/baremetal/nc110-agentos.key && S "chmod 600 /boot/baremetal/nc110-agentos.key" || exit 2
printf '%s\n' "$ENTRY" | S "cat >> /boot/grub/grub.cfg" || { S "cp -p $BK /boot/grub/grub.cfg"; exit 2; }
ESHA=$(printf '%s\n' "$ENTRY" | shasum -a 256 | cut -c1-64); ELEN=$(printf '%s\n' "$ENTRY" | wc -c | tr -d ' ')
v=$(S "sync; sha256sum /boot/baremetal/$KN | cut -c1-64; sha256sum /boot/baremetal/nc110-agentos.key | cut -c1-64; head -c \$(stat -c %s $BK) /boot/grub/grub.cfg | sha256sum | cut -c1-64; sha256sum $BK | cut -c1-64; tail -c $ELEN /boot/grub/grub.cfg | sha256sum | cut -c1-64; grub-script-check /boot/grub/grub.cfg && echo check-ok")
mapfile -t vv <<<"$v"
[ "${vv[0]}" = "$(shasum -a 256 "$K" | cut -c1-64)" ] && [ "${vv[1]}" = "$(shasum -a 256 "$KEY" | cut -c1-64)" ] && [ "${vv[2]}" = "${vv[3]}" ] \
  && [ "${vv[4]}" = "$ESHA" ] && [ "${vv[5]:-}" = check-ok ] || { echo "verify FAILED, restoring grub.cfg"; S "cp -p $BK /boot/grub/grub.cfg; sync"; exit 2; }
echo "installed $E (default unchanged). Test once: next_entry=$E; make default: $0 --set-default $H"
# Appended once to the NC110 grub.cfg (2026-10-18). Defines the GRUB-side TCO arming used by nc110-bm-* entries
# made with GRUBARM=1. lock_mode=accept: the NC110 BIOS sets TCO_LOCK=1/TCO_EN=1; the gate in m1-nc110-entry.cfg
# ("only after a reset at ~2x heartbeat on THIS board") is met by k5/k6/k9 on 2026-10-17: the same register state
# (NO_REBOOT cleared, TCO_EN left set, TMR programmed, timer running) reset the board unaided 60 s after a hang.
function nc110_tco_consts {
  insmod iorw
  insmod memrw
  insmod setpci
  insmod regexp
  search --no-floppy --fs-uuid --set=root c0954bc7-86a0-440c-8401-2c3e95549743
  source /baremetal/tco-arm.cfg
  set lpc_expect=27bc8086
  set pmbase_expect=1001
  set rcba_expect=fed1c001
  set gcs_addr=0xfed1f410
  set smi_en=0x1030
  set tco_rld=0x1060
  set tco1_sts=0x1064
  set tco2_sts=0x1066
  set tco1_cnt=0x1068
  set tco_tmr=0x1072
  set tmr_val=0x64
  set tmr_re='^([0-9a-f]*[048c])?64$'
  set lock_mode=accept
}
# Shared iTCO v2 arm + verify for GRUB 2 (needs insmod iorw memrw setpci regexp). Used verbatim by the QEMU
# rehearsal (grub-test/iso/boot/grub/grub.cfg) and the proposed NC110 entry (grub-test/m1-nc110-entry.cfg).
# Caller sets: lpc_expect pmbase_expect rcba_expect gcs_addr smi_en tco_rld tco1_sts tco2_sts tco1_cnt tco_tmr
#              tmr_val tmr_re. GRUB prints values as %x (lowercase, no 0x, no padding); bit tests are regexps.
# Optional caller override: lock_mode (default refuse, set below). Callers source this file FIRST, then set it.
#   Policy is consumed per call: tco_arm resets lock_mode to refuse first thing, so set it before EACH call.
#   refuse : TCO_LOCK set -> do not arm (the original M1 design: it needs SMI_EN.TCO_EN cleared).
#   accept : TCO_LOCK set -> arm anyway, preserving TCO_LOCK and never writing SMI_EN: TCO_EN is not checked, so the
#            first timeout raises an SMI to the firmware and the reset relies on the second timeout. Only valid
#            once a hardware test showed this firmware's SMI handler does NOT swallow the timeout (NC110 step 1).
#            Linux iTCO_wdt does the same on v2 by default (turn_SMI_watchdog_clear_off=1 < version 2).
set lock_mode=refuse
# tco_arm sets wdt_ok=yes ONLY if every live predicate holds. The caller must boot only inside that branch.

# Bit predicates on %x strings:
#   GCS bit 5 (NO_REBOOT) clear      : nibble 1 in {0,1,4,5,8,9,c,d}
#   TCO1_CNT bit 11 (TMR_HLT) clear  : nibble 2 < 8
#   TCO1_CNT bit 12 (TCO_LOCK) clear : nibble 3 even
#   SMI_EN bit 13 (TCO_EN) clear     : nibble 3 in {0,1,4,5,8,9,c,d}
set re_noreboot_clear='^([0-9a-f]|[0-9a-f]*[014589cd][0-9a-f])$'
set re_hlt_clear='^([0-9a-f]{1,2}|[0-7][0-9a-f]{2}|[0-9a-f]*[0-9a-f][0-7][0-9a-f]{2})$'
set re_lock_clear='^([0-9a-f]{1,3}|[0-9a-f]*[02468ace][0-9a-f]{3})$'
set re_tcoen_clear='^([0-9a-f]{1,3}|[0-9a-f]*[014589cd][0-9a-f]{3})$'
set re_hex16='^[0-9a-f]{1,4}$'

function tco_re_check {
  # $1 = regexp, $2 = value, $3 = expected (yes/no). Sets re_bad=yes on a wrong answer.
  if regexp "$1" "$2"; then set got=yes; else set got=no; fi
  if [ "$got" != "$3" ]; then echo "wdt: selftest FAILED: $2 vs $1 gave $got"; set re_bad=yes; fi
}

function tco_selftest {
  # Proves THIS GRUB's regexp/test behave as the predicates assume, before they guard anything.
  set re_bad=no
  tco_re_check "$re_noreboot_clear" "0" yes
  tco_re_check "$re_noreboot_clear" "fed1f401" yes
  tco_re_check "$re_noreboot_clear" "20" no
  tco_re_check "$re_noreboot_clear" "fed1f421" no
  tco_re_check "$re_hlt_clear" "0" yes
  tco_re_check "$re_hlt_clear" "1200" yes
  tco_re_check "$re_hlt_clear" "800" no
  tco_re_check "$re_hlt_clear" "1a00" no
  tco_re_check "$re_lock_clear" "800" yes
  tco_re_check "$re_lock_clear" "2000" yes
  tco_re_check "$re_lock_clear" "1000" no
  tco_re_check "$re_lock_clear" "3800" no
  tco_re_check "$re_tcoen_clear" "21" yes
  tco_re_check "$re_tcoen_clear" "1021" yes
  tco_re_check "$re_tcoen_clear" "2021" no
  tco_re_check "$re_tcoen_clear" "f021" no
  tco_re_check "$re_hex16" "1000" yes
  tco_re_check "$re_hex16" "unset" no
  tco_re_check "$re_hex16" "11000" no
  tco_re_check '^5$' "5" yes
  tco_re_check '^5$' "4005" no
  if [ "a" != "a" -o "b" != "c" ]; then set got=yes; else set got=no; fi
  if [ "$got" != "yes" ]; then echo "wdt: selftest FAILED: test -o"; set re_bad=yes; fi
  if [ "$re_bad" = "no" ]; then echo "wdt: selftest OK (21 regexp cases + test -o)"; fi
}

function tco_arm {
  set wdt_ok=no
  set tco_mode="$lock_mode"
  set lock_mode=refuse
  tco_selftest
  if [ "$re_bad" != "no" ]; then echo "wdt: GRUB predicates untrustworthy, NOT arming"; return 1; fi
  set lpcid=unset; set pmbase=unset; set rcba=unset
  setpci -s 00:1f.0 -v lpcid 0.l
  setpci -s 00:1f.0 -v pmbase 40.l
  setpci -s 00:1f.0 -v rcba f0.l
  echo "wdt: lpcid=$lpcid pmbase=$pmbase rcba=$rcba"
  if [ "$lpcid" != "$lpc_expect" -o "$pmbase" != "$pmbase_expect" -o "$rcba" != "$rcba_expect" ]; then
    echo "wdt: chipset mismatch, NOT arming"; return 1
  fi
  set cntpre=unset; set sts2pre=unset; set gcspre=unset
  inw -v cntpre $tco1_cnt
  inw -v sts2pre $tco2_sts
  read_dword -v gcspre $gcs_addr
  echo "wdt: before: GCS=$gcspre TCO1_CNT=$cntpre TCO2_STS=$sts2pre (6 = last reset was the watchdog)"
  if regexp "$re_hex16" "$cntpre"; then true; else echo "wdt: TCO1_CNT read invalid ($cntpre), NOT arming"; return 1; fi
  if regexp "$re_lock_clear" "$cntpre"; then set tco_locked=no; else set tco_locked=yes; fi
  if [ "$tco_locked" = "yes" ]; then
    if [ "$tco_mode" != "accept" ]; then echo "wdt: TCO_LOCK set, NOT arming (lock_mode=$tco_mode)"; return 1; fi
    echo "wdt: TCO_LOCK set, lock_mode=accept: TCO_EN left as read, 1st timeout goes to firmware SMI, reset on 2nd"
  fi
  write_dword $gcs_addr 0 0x20       # GCS: clear NO_REBOOT (bit 5), masked RMW
  if [ "$tco_locked" = "no" ]; then
    outl $smi_en 0 0x2000            # SMI_EN: clear TCO_EN (bit 13), masked RMW; locked: never written
  fi
  outw $tco1_sts 0x0008              # TCO1_STS: clear TIMEOUT (RW1C)
  outw $tco2_sts 0x0002              # TCO2_STS: clear SECOND_TO_STS first ...
  outw $tco2_sts 0x0004              # ... then BOOT_STS (Intel order)
  outw $tco_tmr $tmr_val 0x03ff      # TCO_TMR: ticks of 0.6 s in bits 9:0 only
  outw $tco_rld 1                    # TCO_RLD: reload
  outw $tco1_cnt 0 0x0800            # TCO1_CNT: clear TCO_TMR_HLT (bit 11), masked RMW; bit 12 TCO_LOCK written back as read
  set gcs=unset; set cnt=unset; set tmr=unset; set smien=unset; set c0=unset; set c1=unset
  read_dword -v gcs $gcs_addr
  inw -v cnt $tco1_cnt
  inw -v tmr $tco_tmr
  inl -v smien $smi_en
  inw -v c0 $tco_rld
  sleep 1
  inw -v c1 $tco_rld
  echo "wdt: after: GCS=$gcs TCO1_CNT=$cnt TCO_TMR=$tmr SMI_EN=$smien count $c0 -> $c1 locked=$tco_locked mode=$tco_mode"
  if regexp "$re_noreboot_clear" "$gcs"; then true; else echo "wdt: NO_REBOOT still set"; return 1; fi
  if regexp "$re_hlt_clear" "$cnt"; then true; else echo "wdt: timer still halted"; return 1; fi
  if [ "$tco_locked" = "no" ]; then
    if regexp "$re_tcoen_clear" "$smien"; then true; else echo "wdt: SMI_EN.TCO_EN still set"; return 1; fi
  fi
  if regexp "$tmr_re" "$tmr"; then true; else echo "wdt: TCO_TMR not $tmr_val"; return 1; fi
  if [ "$c0" = "$c1" -o "$c0" = "unset" -o "$c1" = "unset" ]; then echo "wdt: counter not moving"; return 1; fi
  set wdt_ok=yes
}
# NC110 agent OS as the default boot (2026-10-18 22:43-23:00, evnchn's go 22:43, ask A179-1018-13)

//! In-RAM chain loader, driven over bmctl (the DS OTA equivalent: nothing is written to /boot).
//! `lbeg <size> <sha256>` opens a staging buffer at STAGE; binary `ldat` ++ u32 offset ++ bytes appends in order;
//! `lcom <cmdline>` verifies length + SHA-256 + the multiboot header (a.out kludge, flag bit 16, as GRUB applies it),
//! checks every destination range, builds a multiboot info block (cmdline, the ORIGINAL GRUB memory map, and a
//! rebuilt 72-byte key module with this boot's seed), quiesces the NIC and jumps through the trampoline in boot.s,
//! which turns paging off, copies the image to load_addr, zeroes its bss and enters it like GRUB would.
//! The TCO stays armed across the hop (the attended successor re-arms it in kmain). Any refusal leaves us running.
use crate::kprintln;
use crate::mb;
use crate::nic::Rtl;
use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};

pub const STAGE: u64 = 0x0400_0000; // 64 MiB
pub const CAP: u64 = 0x0080_0000; // 8 MiB (STAGE + CAP < 128 MiB, so q35 -m 128 works)
const TRAMP: u64 = 0x8000; // trampoline code + its stack (< 0xC00)
const PARAMS: u64 = 0x8C00;
const INFO: u64 = 0x8D00;
const MODENT: u64 = 0x8E80;
const CMDLINE: u64 = 0x8F00; // <= 255 bytes + NUL
const MODULE: u64 = 0x9000; // page-aligned (our header asks for it: flag bit 0)
const MMAP: u64 = 0x9100;
const LOW_END: u64 = 0xA000;
const MMAP_MAX: usize = (LOW_END - MMAP) as usize;

/// GRUB's boot metadata, copied out at boot (before any staging could overwrite it). (flags, mem_lower, mem_upper)
static mut ORIG: (u32, u32, u32) = (0, 0, 0);
static mut ORIG_MMAP: [u8; MMAP_MAX] = [0; MMAP_MAX];
static mut ORIG_MMAP_LEN: usize = 0;
static mut ORIG_MMAP_TOO_BIG: bool = false;

/// Call once, early, with the multiboot info pointer GRUB (or a previous loader) gave us.
pub fn snapshot_boot_info(info: u64) {
    if info == 0 {
        return;
    }
    unsafe {
        let r32 = |a: u64| core::ptr::read_volatile(a as *const u32);
        let f = r32(info);
        ORIG = (f, if f & 1 != 0 { r32(info + 4) } else { 0 }, if f & 1 != 0 { r32(info + 8) } else { 0 });
        if f & (1 << 6) != 0 {
            let (l, a) = (r32(info + 44) as usize, r32(info + 48) as u64);
            if l <= MMAP_MAX {
                core::ptr::copy(a as *const u8, core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(ORIG_MMAP) as *mut u8, l);
                ORIG_MMAP_LEN = l;
            } else {
                ORIG_MMAP_TOO_BIG = true;
            }
        }
    }
}

extern "C" {
    static tramp_start: u8;
    static tramp_end: u8;
    static __load_start: u8;
    static __bss_end: u8;
}

pub struct Stage {
    size: u64,
    sha: [u8; 32],
    got: u64,
}

fn overlaps(a: u64, al: u64, b: u64, bl: u64) -> bool {
    a < b.saturating_add(bl) && b < a.saturating_add(al)
}

fn kernel_image() -> (u64, u64) {
    let (s, e) = (core::ptr::addr_of!(__load_start) as u64, core::ptr::addr_of!(__bss_end) as u64);
    (s, e - s)
}

pub fn begin(size: u64, sha_hex: &str) -> Result<Stage, &'static str> {
    if size == 0 || size > CAP {
        return Err("size 1..8 MiB");
    }
    let (ks, kl) = kernel_image();
    if !mb::ram_contains(STAGE, size) || overlaps(STAGE, size, ks, kl) {
        return Err("staging area not free RAM");
    }
    if sha_hex.len() != 64 || !sha_hex.bytes().all(|c| c.is_ascii_hexdigit()) {
        return Err("sha256: 64 hex chars");
    }
    let mut sha = [0u8; 32];
    for (i, b) in sha.iter_mut().enumerate() {
        *b = u8::from_str_radix(&sha_hex[2 * i..2 * i + 2], 16).map_err(|_| "sha256: hex")?;
    }
    Ok(Stage { size, sha, got: 0 })
}

pub fn data(st: &mut Stage, off: u64, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<u64, &'static str> {
    if off != st.got {
        return Err("out of order (send chunks in sequence)");
    }
    let end = off.checked_add(bytes.len() as u64).ok_or("overflow")?;
    if end > st.size {
        return Err("past announced size");
    }
    unsafe { core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(bytes.as_ptr(), (STAGE + off) as *mut u8, bytes.len()) };
    st.got = end;
    Ok(end)
}

/// Everything checked, nothing moved yet. Returns (src, dst, len, bss_len, entry).
fn plan(st: &Stage) -> Result<(u64, u64, u64, u64, u64), &'static str> {
    if st.got != st.size {
        return Err("incomplete image");
    }
    let img = unsafe { core::slice::from_raw_parts(STAGE as *const u8, st.size as usize) };
    let d: [u8; 32] = Sha256::digest(img).into();
    if d != st.sha {
        return Err("sha256 mismatch");
    }
    let rd = |o: usize| u32::from_le_bytes(img[o..o + 4].try_into().unwrap());
    let lim = img.len().min(8192);
    let hoff = (0..lim.saturating_sub(31)).step_by(4).find(|&o| {
        rd(o) == 0x1BAD_B002 && rd(o).wrapping_add(rd(o + 4)).wrapping_add(rd(o + 8)) == 0
    }).ok_or("no multiboot header in the first 8 KiB")?;
    let flags = rd(hoff + 4);
    if flags & (1 << 16) == 0 {
        return Err("multiboot header lacks the address kludge (flag 16)");
    }
    if flags & 0xFFFC != 0 {
        return Err("multiboot header requires features this loader does not provide (bits 2..15)");
    }
    let (haddr, laddr, lend, bend, entry) =
        (rd(hoff + 12) as u64, rd(hoff + 16) as u64, rd(hoff + 20) as u64, rd(hoff + 24) as u64, rd(hoff + 28) as u64);
    if laddr > haddr || lend <= laddr || bend < lend || entry < laddr || entry >= lend {
        return Err("inconsistent header addresses");
    }
    let foff = (hoff as u64).checked_sub(haddr - laddr).ok_or("header offset")?;
    let len = lend - laddr;
    if foff.checked_add(len).is_none_or(|e| e > st.size) {
        return Err("image shorter than load_end - load_addr");
    }
    if laddr < 0x10_0000 || bend > STAGE || !mb::ram_contains(laddr, bend - laddr) {
        return Err("target range not RAM in [1 MiB, staging)");
    }
    if overlaps(laddr, bend - laddr, TRAMP, LOW_END - TRAMP) {
        return Err("target overlaps the trampoline/info pages");
    }
    Ok((STAGE + foff, laddr, len, bend - lend, entry))
}

pub struct Plan {
    params: [u64; 6],
}

/// All checks plus writing the low pages (trampoline, info, module, cmdline, mmap). Nothing of ours is touched:
/// the low pages are outside the kernel image; GRUB's info/mmap come from the boot-time snapshot.
pub fn prepare(st: &Stage, cmdline: &str, seed: &[u8; 32]) -> Result<Plan, &'static str> {
    let (src, dst, len, bss, entry) = plan(st)?;
    let tl = (core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_end) as u64) - (core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_start) as u64);
    if tl > PARAMS - TRAMP || !mb::ram_contains(TRAMP, LOW_END - TRAMP) {
        return Err("low pages 0x8000-0xA000 not usable");
    }
    if cmdline.len() > 255 {
        return Err("cmdline > 255");
    }
    unsafe {
        if ORIG_MMAP_TOO_BIG {
            return Err("GRUB memory map larger than the loader's buffer: refusing rather than dropping it");
        }
        let (oflags, mlo, mhi) = ORIG;
        let ml = ORIG_MMAP_LEN;
        let mmap = &*core::ptr::addr_of!(ORIG_MMAP);
        let w32 = |a: u64, v: u32| core::ptr::write_volatile(a as *mut u32, v);
        core::ptr::write_bytes(TRAMP as *mut u8, 0, (LOW_END - TRAMP) as usize);
        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_start), TRAMP as *mut u8, tl as usize);
        // key module: MAGIC + seed + public key, exactly the 72 bytes GRUB's `module` line gave us
        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(mb::MAGIC.as_ptr(), MODULE as *mut u8, 8);
        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(seed.as_ptr(), (MODULE + 8) as *mut u8, 32);
        let publ = sunset::ed25519_dalek::SigningKey::from_bytes(seed).verifying_key().to_bytes();
        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(publ.as_ptr(), (MODULE + 40) as *mut u8, 32);
        w32(MODENT, MODULE as u32);
        w32(MODENT + 4, (MODULE + 72) as u32);
        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(cmdline.as_ptr(), CMDLINE as *mut u8, cmdline.len());
        // multiboot info: flags 0 (mem), 2 (cmdline), 3 (mods), 6 (GRUB's memory map, verbatim)
        let mut flags = (1 << 2) | (1 << 3);
        if oflags & 1 != 0 {
            flags |= 1;
            w32(INFO + 4, mlo);
            w32(INFO + 8, mhi);
        }
        if ml > 0 {
            core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(mmap.as_ptr(), MMAP as *mut u8, ml);
            flags |= 1 << 6;
            w32(INFO + 44, ml as u32);
            w32(INFO + 48, MMAP as u32);
        }
        w32(INFO, flags);
        w32(INFO + 16, CMDLINE as u32);
        w32(INFO + 20, 1);
        w32(INFO + 24, MODENT as u32);
    }
    kprintln!("loader: ready: {} B image -> {:#x}..{:#x} (+{} B bss), entry {:#x}, cmdline '{}'", st.size, dst, dst + len, bss, entry, cmdline);
    Ok(Plan { params: [src, dst, len, bss, entry, INFO] })
}

/// Point of no return: NIC quiesced (reset completed), trampoline entered. The TCO stays armed across the hop.
/// If the NIC reset does not complete, its DMA may still target memory the new image would overwrite: hard reset
/// instead of jumping (back to Slot A via GRUB's default).
pub fn jump(p: &Plan, nic: &Rtl) -> ! {
    if let Err(e) = nic.quiesce() {
        kprintln!("loader: NIC quiesce failed ({}): NOT jumping, hard reset", e);
        crate::wdt::hard_reset();
    }
    unsafe {
        for (i, v) in p.params.iter().enumerate() {
            core::ptr::write_volatile((PARAMS + 8 * i as u64) as *mut u64, *v);
        }
        crate::wdt::pet();
        let f: extern "sysv64" fn(u64) -> ! = core::mem::transmute(TRAMP as usize);
        f(PARAMS)
    }
}

use core::arch::{asm, global_asm};

struct SerialLog;
impl log::Log for SerialLog {
    fn enabled(&self, m: &log::Metadata) -> bool {
        m.level() <= log::Level::Warn
    }
    fn log(&self, r: &log::Record) {
        if self.enabled(r.metadata()) {
            kprintln!("[{}] {}", r.level(), r.args());
        }
    }
    fn flush(&self) {}
}
static LOGGER: SerialLog = SerialLog;

#[no_mangle]
pub extern "C" fn kmain(magic: u32, info: u64) -> ! {
    console::init();
    // Attended: arm the TCO before anything else can block (Codex night review MUST-FIX 1). No net -> no run.
    if cfg!(feature = "attended") && !wdt::arm_probe() {
        kprintln!("wdt: ATTENDED: could not arm the TCO, refusing to run");
        wdt::hard_reset();
    }
    kprintln!("NC110 bare-metal M3: hello from our own x86_64 kernel (Rust, no_std)");
    if let Some(b) = option_env!("NC110_BUILD") {
        kprintln!("build: {}", b);
    }
    kprintln!("multiboot magic={:#010x} info={:#x} -> long mode OK", magic, info);
    wdt::report();
    if cfg!(feature = "attended") {
        kprintln!("wdt: ATTENDED build: TCO armed by us with NO_REBOOT cleared (one-shot boot, Slot A on any reset)");
    } else if !wdt::is_qemu_pc() && !wdt::verify_live() && !wdt::neg_control_on_q35() {
        kprintln!("wdt: reset path not verified on this machine, refusing to run");
        wdt::hard_reset();
    }
    // Key module right after the watchdog gate: copy the seed out and wipe the module before anything else.
    let client_seed = mb::take_client_key(info);
    mb::parse_memory(info);
    let cmdline = mb::cmdline(info);
    kprintln!("cmdline: {}", cmdline);
    wdt::rehearse(cmdline);

    let _ = log::set_logger(&LOGGER).map(|()| log::set_max_level(log::LevelFilter::Warn));
    kprintln!("time: TSC {} ticks/ms (PIT ch2 calibrated)", time::init());

    match nic::probe(cmdline.contains("nic=mmio")) {
        Ok(mut n) => {
            if cmdline.contains("nic=no8105e") {
                n.skip_8105e(); // A/B switch for the rtl_hw_start_8105e tweaks
            }
            kprintln!("nic: {} at {} {} mac={}", n.name(), n.bdf, n.bus, dhcp::Mac(n.mac));
            match n.init() {
                Ok(()) if cfg!(feature = "attended") => {
                    kprintln!("udplog: broadcasting this log to UDP 5514");
                    udplog::set_nic(&n);
                    udplog::dump(1);
                    udplog::dhcp_probe(&mut n);
                    udplog::dump(2);
                    if cmdline.contains("wdt=probe") {
                        wdt::pet();
                        kprintln!("wdt: HANG now (probe): cli; hlt, nobody pets; expect a reset ~60 s from here");
                        udplog::dump(3);
                        loop {
                            unsafe { asm!("cli; hlt") };
                        }
                    }
                    if let (Some(seed), true) = (client_seed, cmdline.contains("bmctl")) {
                        bmctl::run(n, seed, info);
                    } else if client_seed.is_some() {
                        ssh::run(n, client_seed, cmdline.contains("rng=dump"));
                    }
                    kprintln!("attended: final log round, then reset");
                    udplog::dump(4);
                }
                Ok(()) => ssh::run(n, client_seed, cmdline.contains("rng=dump")),
                Err(e) => kprintln!("nic: init failed: {} (bus mastering left off)", e),
            }
        }
        Err(e) => kprintln!("nic: {}", e),
    }
    kprintln!("DONE");
    if cfg!(feature = "attended") {
        kprintln!("attended: hard reset via 0xCF9 in 60 s");
        for _ in 0..60 {
            wdt::pet();
            wdt::delay_ms(1000);
        }
        wdt::hard_reset();
    }
    loop {
        unsafe { asm!("cli; hlt") };
    }
}

}

    let _ = writeln!(console::Console, "PANIC: {}", info);
    loop {
        unsafe { asm!("cli; hlt") };
    }
}
            let _ = core::fmt::write(w, format_args!("{:#x}", v));
        }
        _ => return Err("peek mem <hexaddr> <len> | peek nic <hexoff> <len> | peek io <hexport> <1|2|4>"),
    }
    Ok(())

pub fn run(nic: Rtl, seed: [u8; 32], info: u64) {
    let idle_ms = crate::mb::cmdline(info).split(' ').find_map(|w| w.strip_prefix("idle=")).and_then(|v| v.parse::<i64>().ok())
        .filter(|v| (5..=3600).contains(v)).map_or(IDLE_MS, |v| v * 1000);
    crate::loader::snapshot_boot_info(info);
    let nic_mmio = match nic.bus {
        Bus::Mmio(b) => Some(b),
        Bus::Pio(_) => None,
    };
    if rng::init(false).is_err() {
        return;
    }
    let key = derive_key(&seed);
    let mut boot_id = [0u8; 16];
    let mut rs = [0u8; 8];
    if rng::fill(&mut boot_id).is_err() || rng::fill(&mut rs).is_err() {
        kprintln!("bmctl: entropy failed -> not starting");
        return;
    }
    let mut bid = heapless::String::<64>::new();
    hex(&boot_id, &mut bid);
    let mac = nic.mac;
    let mut dev = Nic(nic);
    let mut cfg = Config::new(HardwareAddress::Ethernet(EthernetAddress(mac)));
    cfg.random_seed = u64::from_le_bytes(rs);
    let mut iface = Interface::new(cfg, &mut dev, now());
    let mut sockets = SocketSet::new(unsafe { &mut *core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(SOCKS) }[..].as_mut());
    let dhcp_h = sockets.add(dhcpv4::Socket::new());
    let udp_h = sockets.add(udp::Socket::new(
        udp::PacketBuffer::new(unsafe { &mut *core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(RX_META) }[..].as_mut(), unsafe { &mut *core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(RX_BUF) }[..].as_mut()),
        udp::PacketBuffer::new(unsafe { &mut *core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(TX_META) }[..].as_mut(), unsafe { &mut *core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(TX_BUF) }[..].as_mut()),
    ));
    let t0 = time::now_ms();
    let mut ip = None;
    while ip.is_none() && time::now_ms() - t0 < 15_000 {
        wdt::pet();
        crate::net::rx_budget(Some(32));
        iface.poll(now(), &mut dev, &mut sockets);
        if let Some(dhcpv4::Event::Configured(c)) = sockets.get_mut::<dhcpv4::Socket>(dhcp_h).poll() {
            iface.update_ip_addrs(|a| {
                a.clear();
                let _ = a.push(IpCidr::Ipv4(c.address));
            });
            if let Some(gw) = c.router {
                let _ = iface.routes_mut().add_default_ipv4_route(gw);
            }
            ip = Some(c.address);
        }
    }
    let Some(ip) = ip else {
        kprintln!("bmctl: no DHCP lease in 15 s -> not starting");
        return;
    };
    sockets.get_mut::<udp::Socket>(udp_h).bind(PORT).expect("bind");
    kprintln!("bmctl: listening on {}:{} boot id {} (idle reset after {} s)", ip.address(), PORT, bid, idle_ms / 1000);
    crate::udplog::dump(5);

    let (mut last_ok, mut last_hello) = (time::now_ms(), 0i64);
    let (mut ok, mut bad, mut replay, mut retx) = (0u32, 0u32, 0u32, 0u32);
    let (mut next_sess, mut who_window, mut who_count) = (1u32, 0i64, 0u32);
    let sess = unsafe { &mut *core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(SESS) };
    let pend = unsafe { &mut *core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(PEND) };
    let mut reboot_at: Option<i64> = None;
    let mut stage: Option<crate::loader::Stage> = None;
    let mut jump_at: Option<(i64, crate::loader::Plan)> = None;
    loop {
        wdt::pet();
        crate::net::rx_budget(Some(32)); // bounded ingress per poll so the deadline checks below always run
        iface.poll(now(), &mut dev, &mut sockets);
        let t = time::now_ms();
        if t - last_ok > idle_ms {
            kprintln!("bmctl: idle {} s -> reset", idle_ms / 1000);
            return;
        }
        if jump_at.as_ref().is_some_and(|(j, _)| t > *j) {
            let (_, p) = jump_at.take().unwrap();
            kprintln!("bmctl: chain-loading the staged kernel");
            crate::udplog::dump(7);
            crate::loader::jump(&p, &dev.0);
        }
        if reboot_at.is_some_and(|r| t > r) {
            kprintln!("bmctl: reboot requested");
            wdt::hard_reset();
        }
        let sock = sockets.get_mut::<udp::Socket>(udp_h);
        if t - last_hello > 2000 {
            last_hello = t;
            let mut h = heapless::String::<128>::new();
            let _ = core::fmt::write(&mut h, format_args!("nc110 hello bmctl {}:{} boot {}", ip.address(), PORT, bid));
            let _ = sock.send_slice(h.as_bytes(), IpEndpoint::new(IpAddress::Ipv4(Ipv4Address::BROADCAST), 5514));
        }
        let mut buf = [0u8; 1500];
        let Ok((n, meta)) = sock.recv_slice(&mut buf) else { continue };
        let from = meta.endpoint;
        if n == 3 + 16 && &buf[..3] == b"who" {
            if t - who_window > 1000 {
                (who_window, who_count) = (t, 0);
            }
            who_count += 1;
            if who_count > 10 {
                continue; // global cap: at most 10 clear-text replies per second (reflection)
            }
            // Unauthenticated `who` only ever writes the pending table (round-robin), never an authenticated session.
            let slot = Some((next_sess as usize) % pend.len());
            let (Some(slot), Some(next)) = (slot, next_sess.checked_add(1)) else { continue };
            let id = next_sess;
            next_sess = next;
            let mut nc = [0u8; 16];
            nc.copy_from_slice(&buf[3..19]);
            pend[slot] = Some(Sess { id, key: session_key(&key, &boot_id, id, &nc), last_seq: 0, peer: Some(from), last_t: t, retx: 0, reply: [0; 8 + 1300 + 16], reply_len: 0 });
            let mut chk = heapless::String::<64>::new();
            hex(&who_check(&key, &nc, &boot_id, id)[..16], &mut chk);
            let mut h = heapless::String::<160>::new();
            let _ = core::fmt::write(&mut h, format_args!("nc110 boot {} session {} check {}", bid, id, chk));
            let _ = sock.send_slice(h.as_bytes(), from);
            continue;
        }
        // Frame: [session u32 LE][seq u64 LE][ct][tag]
        if n < 4 + 8 + 16 {
            bad += 1;
            continue;
        }
        let sid = u32::from_le_bytes(buf[..4].try_into().unwrap());
        // Authenticated session, or a pending one from the endpoint that asked `who` (promoted below once the frame
        // authenticates; only then may it evict the least recently used authenticated session).
        let in_sess = sess.iter().position(|x| x.as_ref().is_some_and(|x| x.id == sid && x.peer == Some(from)));
        let in_pend = pend.iter().position(|x| x.as_ref().is_some_and(|x| x.id == sid && x.peer == Some(from)));
        let se = match (in_sess, in_pend) {
            (Some(i), _) => sess[i].as_mut().unwrap(),
            (None, Some(i)) => pend[i].as_mut().unwrap(),
            _ => {
                bad += 1;
                continue;
            }
        };
        let seq = u64::from_le_bytes(buf[4..12].try_into().unwrap());
        let (body, tg) = buf[12..n].split_at_mut(n - 12 - 16);
        if !open(&se.key, &nonce(b"C2K\0", seq), &boot_id, body, tg) {
            bad += 1;
            continue;
        }
        if seq == se.last_seq && se.reply_len > 0 {
            if se.retx >= 5 {
                continue; // cap resends of one reply
            }
            se.retx += 1;
            retx += 1; // retry of the last request: same bytes again (to the bound peer), nothing re-executed
            let _ = sock.send_slice(&se.reply[..se.reply_len], from);
            continue;
        }
        if seq <= se.last_seq {
            replay += 1;
            continue;
        }
        se.last_seq = seq;
        se.last_t = t;
        se.retx = 0;
        last_ok = t;
        let se = if let (None, Some(i)) = (in_sess, in_pend) {
            let dst = (0..sess.len()).find(|&j| sess[j].is_none())
                .unwrap_or_else(|| (0..sess.len()).min_by_key(|&j| sess[j].as_ref().map_or(i64::MIN, |x| x.last_t)).unwrap());
            sess[dst] = pend[i].take();
            sess[dst].as_mut().unwrap()
        } else {
            se
        };
        ok += 1;
        let mut out = [0u8; 1300];
        let len = {
            let mut w = Writer { b: &mut out, n: 0 };
            let loading = |c: &[u8]| c.starts_with(b"ldat") || c.starts_with(b"lbeg") || c.starts_with(b"lcom");
            if jump_at.is_some() && loading(body) {
                // committed: the staged image is frozen until the hop (Codex loader MUST-FIX 2)
                let _ = core::fmt::write(&mut w, format_args!("err committed, jump pending"));
            } else if body.len() >= 8 && &body[..4] == b"ldat" {
//! TCO watchdog (ICH9 in QEMU q35, NM10 on the NC110; both iTCO v2). boot.s arms it before any
//! Rust runs; this module reports what boot.s did, pets it, and runs the M0.5 rehearsal modes.
use crate::port::{inl, inw, outb, outw};
use crate::{kprintln, pci};
use core::ptr::{addr_of, read_volatile};

#[cfg(all(feature = "neg-control", feature = "grub-armed"))]
compile_error!("neg-control is QEMU-only and must never be combined with grub-armed (the hardware artifact)");

/// Negative control (`--features neg-control`, QEMU q35 only; boot.s never self-arms on NM10): boot.s SETS GCS.NO_REBOOT instead of clearing it
/// (QEMU's GCS powers up 0 with noreboot=false, so merely skipping the clear would prove nothing).
#[no_mangle]
static TCO_NEG_CONTROL: u8 = cfg!(feature = "neg-control") as u8;

/// `--features grub-armed`: boot.s does NOT arm; used to test the GRUB iorw/memrw arming sequence.
#[no_mangle]
static TCO_GRUB_ARMED: u8 = cfg!(feature = "grub-armed") as u8;

extern "C" {
    static tco_result: u32;
    static tco_gcs_pre: u32;
    static tco_gcs: u32;
    static tco_base: u32;
    static tco_sts1_pre: u16;
    static tco_sts2_pre: u16;
    static tco_cnt: u16;
}

fn rd<T: Copy>(p: *const T) -> T {
    unsafe { read_volatile(p) }
}

pub fn armed() -> bool {
    matches!(rd(addr_of!(tco_result)), 0 | 4 | 5)
}

pub fn pmbase() -> u16 {
    (pci::read32(pci::b0(31, 0), 0x40) & 0xFF80) as u16
}

pub fn base() -> u16 {
    pmbase() + 0x60
}

pub fn report() {
    let r = rd(addr_of!(tco_result));
    let what = match r {
        0 => "armed by boot.s",
        1 => "skipped (LPC 00:1f.0 is not ICH9 8086:2918 / NM10 8086:27bc)",
        2 => "NOT armed: PMBASE unset",
        3 => "NOT armed: RCBA disabled",
        4 => "left to GRUB (grub-armed build); live registers follow",
        5 => "NM10: boot.s does not arm (attended Rust arms it; else GRUB must); live registers follow",
        _ => "NOT armed: unknown",
    };
    kprintln!("wdt: {} (result={})", what, r);
    if r == 4 || r == 5 {
        let b = base();
        let gcs = unsafe { read_volatile(((pci::read32(pci::b0(31, 0), 0xF0) & 0xFFFF_C000) + 0x3410) as *const u32) };
        let (s1, s2, cnt, tmr) = unsafe { (inw(b + 4), inw(b + 6), inw(b + 8), inw(b + 0x12)) };
        kprintln!(
            "wdt: live TCOBASE={:#x} GCS={:#010x} NO_REBOOT={} TCO1_CNT={:#06x} HLT={} TCO1_STS={:#06x} TCO2_STS={:#06x} TCO_TMR={}",
            b, gcs, (gcs >> 5) & 1, cnt, (cnt >> 11) & 1, s1, s2, tmr & 0x3FF
        );
        return;
    }
    if r != 0 {
        return;
    }
    let (b, gcs, s1, s2, cnt) = {
        (rd(addr_of!(tco_base)) as u16, rd(addr_of!(tco_gcs)), rd(addr_of!(tco_sts1_pre)), rd(addr_of!(tco_sts2_pre)), rd(addr_of!(tco_cnt)))
    };
    kprintln!(
        "wdt: TCOBASE={:#x} GCS {:#010x} -> {:#010x} NO_REBOOT={} TCO1_CNT={:#06x} HLT={} LOCK={}",
        b, rd(addr_of!(tco_gcs_pre)), gcs, (gcs >> 5) & 1, cnt, (cnt >> 11) & 1, (cnt >> 12) & 1
    );
    kprintln!(
        "wdt: before-clear TCO1_STS={:#06x} TCO2_STS={:#06x} SECOND_TO_STS={} BOOT_STS={}",
        s1, s2, (s2 >> 1) & 1, (s2 >> 2) & 1
    );
    let tmr = unsafe { inw(b + 0x12) } & 0x3FF;
    kprintln!("wdt: TCO_TMR={} ticks x 0.6 s, reset expected after 2nd expiry (~{} ms)", tmr, tmr as u32 * 1200);
}

/// Live check of the reset path, whoever armed it: NO_REBOOT=0, HLT=0, TMR>=4, and the counter
/// (TCO_RLD reads back the current value on v2) decrements across ~1.3 ticks.
pub fn verify_live() -> bool {
    if !armed() {
        return false;
    }
    let b = base();
    let rcba = pci::read32(pci::b0(31, 0), 0xF0);
    if rcba & 1 == 0 {
        return false;
    }
    let gcs = unsafe { read_volatile(((rcba & 0xFFFF_C000) + 0x3410) as *const u32) };
    let (cnt, tmr) = unsafe { (inw(b + 8), inw(b + 0x12) & 0x3FF) };
    let c0 = unsafe { inw(b) } & 0x3FF;
    delay_ms(800);
    let c1 = unsafe { inw(b) } & 0x3FF;
    let ok = gcs & 0x20 == 0 && cnt & 0x0800 == 0 && tmr >= 4 && c1 != c0;
    kprintln!(
        "wdt: verify NO_REBOOT={} HLT={} TMR={} count {}->{} => {}",
        (gcs >> 5) & 1, (cnt >> 11) & 1, tmr, c0, c1, if ok { "VERIFIED" } else { "NOT VERIFIED" }
    );
    ok
}

/// QEMU `-machine pc` (PIIX3 8086:7000 at 00:01.0) has no TCO; the M0 NIC demo may run unprotected there.
pub fn is_qemu_pc() -> bool {
    pci::read32(pci::b0(1, 0), 0) == 0x7000_8086 && rd(addr_of!(tco_result)) == 1
}

/// The negative control must reach its hang unverified (that is the point); only on q35, never NM10.
pub fn neg_control_on_q35() -> bool {
    let on = cfg!(feature = "neg-control") && rd(addr_of!(tco_result)) == 0;
    if on {
        kprintln!("wdt: neg-control build on q35: proceeding unverified on purpose");
    }
    on
}

/// Hard reset via the reset control register (spec M2 `reboot`): back to the firmware / Slot A.
pub fn hard_reset() -> ! {
    kprintln!("wdt: hard reset via 0xCF9");
    unsafe {
        outb(0xCF9, 0x02);
        outb(0xCF9, 0x06);
    }
    loop {
        unsafe { core::arch::asm!("cli; hlt") };
    }
}

/// Attended `wdt=probe`: arm the NM10 TCO ourselves without touching locked bits (SMI_EN.TCO_EN stays as the
/// BIOS left it): clear GCS.NO_REBOOT (the BIOS leaves it SET on the NC110), clear stale status, TMR=50 ticks
/// (30 s per stage, reset ~60 s after the last pet), reload, un-halt. Returns true if the readback says armed.
pub fn arm_probe() -> bool {
    let lpc = pci::read32(pci::b0(31, 0), 0);
    let rcba_raw = pci::read32(pci::b0(31, 0), 0xF0);
    if !(lpc == 0x27BC_8086 || lpc == 0x2918_8086) || pmbase() == 0 || rcba_raw & 1 == 0 {
        kprintln!("wdt: probe arm: prerequisites fail (LPC {:#010x}, PMBASE {:#x}, RCBA {:#x})", lpc, pmbase(), rcba_raw);
        return false;
    }
    let b = base();
    let rcba = rcba_raw & 0xFFFF_C000;
    let gcs_p = (rcba + 0x3410) as *mut u32;
    unsafe {
        let g0 = read_volatile(gcs_p);
        let (cnt0, tmr0, rld0) = (inw(b + 8), inw(b + 0x12) & 0x3FF, inw(b) & 0x3FF);
        kprintln!("wdt: inherited: GCS {:#x} NO_REBOOT={} TCO1_CNT={:#06x} HLT={} TMR={} RLD={} ({})", g0, (g0 >> 5) & 1, cnt0,
            (cnt0 >> 11) & 1, tmr0, rld0, if g0 & 0x20 == 0 && cnt0 & 0x800 == 0 { "inherited enabled: NO_REBOOT clear, timer running" } else { "inherited not enabled" });
        core::ptr::write_volatile(gcs_p, g0 & !0x20);
        outw(b + 4, 0x0008); // TCO1_STS.TIMEOUT (RW1C)
        outw(b + 6, 0x0002); // TCO2_STS.SECOND_TO_STS
        outw(b + 6, 0x0004); // TCO2_STS.BOOT_STS
        outw(b + 0x12, (inw(b + 0x12) & !0x3FF) | 50);
        outw(b, 1);
        outw(b + 8, inw(b + 8) & !0x0800); // un-halt; bit 12 (lock) written back as read
        let (g1, cnt, tmr, c0) = (read_volatile(gcs_p), inw(b + 8), inw(b + 0x12) & 0x3FF, inw(b) & 0x3FF);
        delay_ms(1300);
        let c1 = inw(b) & 0x3FF;
        let ok = g1 & 0x20 == 0 && cnt & 0x0800 == 0 && tmr == 50 && c1 != c0;
        kprintln!("wdt: probe arm: GCS {:#x} -> {:#x} TCO1_CNT={:#06x} TMR={} RLD {}->{} SMI_EN={:#x} => {}",
            g0, g1, cnt, tmr, c0, c1, inl(pmbase() + 0x30), if ok { "ARMED" } else { "NOT ARMED" });
        ok
    }
}

pub fn pet() {
    unsafe { outw(base(), 1) };
}

/// ACPI PM timer (PMBASE+0x08, 3.579545 MHz, 24-bit) based delay; same block on ICH9 and NM10.
fn pm_timer() -> u32 {
    unsafe { inl(pmbase() + 8) & 0x00FF_FFFF }
}

pub fn delay_ms(ms: u32) {
    let need = ms as u64 * 3_579_545 / 1000;
    let mut last = pm_timer();
    let mut acc = 0u64;
    while acc < need {
        let now = pm_timer();
        acc += (now.wrapping_sub(last) & 0x00FF_FFFF) as u64;
        last = now;
    }
}

/// `wdt=pet:N` pets every 500 ms for N s, then stops and hangs. `wdt=hang` hangs at once.
pub fn rehearse(cmdline: &str) -> bool {
    let Some(i) = cmdline.find("wdt=") else { return false };
    let arg = cmdline[i + 4..].split(' ').next().unwrap_or("");
    if arg == "probe" {
        return false; // attended net, handled in main (k5 hung here before the NIC: result 5 counts as armed)
    }
    if !armed() {
        kprintln!("wdt: mode {} requested but watchdog not armed", arg);
        return false;
    }
    let secs: u32 = arg.strip_prefix("pet:").and_then(|n| n.parse().ok()).unwrap_or(0);
    for s in 0..secs {
        for _ in 0..2 {
            pet();
            delay_ms(500);
        }
        kprintln!("wdt: alive t={}s (petting every 500 ms)", s + 1);
    }
    kprintln!("wdt: HANG now: cli; hlt with the timer running, nobody pets");
    loop {
        unsafe { core::arch::asm!("cli; hlt") };
    }
}

//! Multiboot v1 info: cmdline, memory map, and the client-key module (M2.5).
//!
//! The SSH client key is NOT compiled into the ELF. GRUB loads it with `module /baremetal/<file>` (QEMU:
//! `-initrd <file>`), so the key lives on /boot and can be rotated without a rebuild.
//! Module format (72 bytes): b"NC110K1\n" | ed25519 seed (32) | ed25519 public key (32).
//! The kernel derives the public key from the seed and refuses on mismatch (truncation / corruption / wrong
//! file), copies the seed into a static, then zeroes the module bytes in RAM.
use crate::kprintln;
use core::ptr::{read_volatile, write_volatile};

pub const MAGIC: &[u8; 8] = b"NC110K1\n";
const KEYMOD_LEN: u64 = 72;

static mut RAM: [(u64, u64); 32] = [(0, 0); 32];
static mut NRAM: usize = 0;
static mut HAVE_MAP: bool = false;

unsafe fn rd32(a: u64) -> u32 {
    read_volatile(a as *const u32)
}

/// Record available-RAM ranges (mmap type 1, or mem_lower/mem_upper as a fallback).
pub fn parse_memory(info: u64) {
    unsafe {
        if info == 0 {
            return;
        }
        let flags = rd32(info);
        if flags & (1 << 6) != 0 {
            let len = rd32(info + 44) as u64;
            let mut p = rd32(info + 48) as u64;
            let end = p + len;
            while p + 24 <= end && NRAM < 32 {
                let size = rd32(p) as u64;
                let base = read_volatile((p + 4) as *const u64);
                let l = read_volatile((p + 12) as *const u64);
                let ty = rd32(p + 20);
                if ty == 1 && l > 0 {
                    RAM[NRAM] = (base, l);
                    NRAM += 1;
                }
                p += size + 4;
            }
            HAVE_MAP = true;
        } else if flags & 1 != 0 {
            RAM[0] = (0, rd32(info + 4) as u64 * 1024);
            RAM[1] = (0x10_0000, rd32(info + 8) as u64 * 1024);
            NRAM = 2;
            HAVE_MAP = true;
        }
    }
}

/// True if [base, base+len) touches available RAM, or if no memory map was given (fail closed).
pub fn ram_overlaps(base: u64, len: u64) -> bool {
    unsafe {
        if !HAVE_MAP {
            return true;
        }
        let ram = &*core::ptr::addr_of!(RAM);
        ram[..NRAM].iter().any(|&(b, l)| base < b + l && b < base + len)
    }
}

/// True only if [base, base+len) lies entirely inside ONE available-RAM range of the multiboot map.
pub fn ram_contains(base: u64, len: u64) -> bool {
    let Some(end) = base.checked_add(len) else { return false };
    unsafe {
        let ram = &*core::ptr::addr_of!(RAM);
        HAVE_MAP && ram[..NRAM].iter().any(|&(b, l)| base >= b && end <= b.saturating_add(l))
    }
}

pub fn cmdline(info: u64) -> &'static str {
    unsafe {
        if info == 0 || rd32(info) & 4 == 0 {
            return "";
        }
        let p = rd32(info + 16) as *const u8;
        let mut n = 0;
        while n < 256 && *p.add(n) != 0 {
            n += 1;
        }
        core::str::from_utf8(core::slice::from_raw_parts(p, n)).unwrap_or("")
    }
}

/// Find the key module, validate it, copy the seed out, wipe the module. None (with a log line) if absent/bad.
pub fn take_client_key(info: u64) -> Option<[u8; 32]> {
    unsafe {
        if info == 0 || rd32(info) & (1 << 3) == 0 || rd32(info + 20) == 0 {
            kprintln!("keymod: no multiboot module");
            return None;
        }
        let count = rd32(info + 20).min(16) as u64;
        let mods = rd32(info + 24) as u64;
        for i in 0..count {
            let e = mods + 16 * i;
            let (start, end) = (rd32(e) as u64, rd32(e + 4) as u64);
            if end <= start || end - start != KEYMOD_LEN {
                continue;
            }
            let m = core::slice::from_raw_parts(start as *const u8, KEYMOD_LEN as usize);
            if &m[..8] != MAGIC {
                continue;
            }
            let mut seed = [0u8; 32];
            let mut publ = [0u8; 32];
            seed.copy_from_slice(&m[8..40]);
            publ.copy_from_slice(&m[40..72]);
            for j in 0..KEYMOD_LEN {
                write_volatile((start + j) as *mut u8, 0);
            }
            let derived = sunset::ed25519_dalek::SigningKey::from_bytes(&seed).verifying_key().to_bytes();
            if derived != publ {
                kprintln!("keymod: module {} public key does not match its seed -> ignored (wiped)", i);
                return None;
            }
            kprintln!("keymod: client key from module {} at {:#x} (72 B), public key verified, module wiped", i, start);
            return Some(seed);
//! Monotonic milliseconds from the TSC, calibrated once against PIT channel 2 (gate via port 0x61).
use crate::port::{inb, outb, rdtsc};

static mut TSC_PER_MS: u64 = 1_000_000; // fallback guess (1 GHz) if calibration fails
static mut T0: u64 = 0;

pub fn init() -> u64 {
    unsafe {
        // PIT ch2, mode 0, lobyte/hibyte, count for 10 ms at 1.193182 MHz.
        const COUNT: u16 = 11932;
        let g = inb(0x61);
        outb(0x61, (g & 0xFD) | 0x01); // gate on, speaker off
        outb(0x43, 0b1011_0000);
        outb(0x42, COUNT as u8);
        outb(0x42, (COUNT >> 8) as u8);
        // Re-trigger: toggle gate low->high so mode 0 starts counting now.
        let g = inb(0x61);
        outb(0x61, g & 0xFE);
        outb(0x61, g | 0x01);
        let a = rdtsc();
        let mut spins: u64 = 0;
        while inb(0x61) & 0x20 == 0 && spins < 200_000_000 {
            spins += 1;
        }
        let b = rdtsc();
        if spins < 200_000_000 && b > a {
            TSC_PER_MS = ((b - a) / 10).max(1);
        }
        T0 = rdtsc();
        TSC_PER_MS
    }
}

pub fn now_ms() -> i64 {
    unsafe { ((rdtsc() - T0) / TSC_PER_MS) as i64 }
}

/// Busy-wait at least `us` microseconds (TSC, after init()).
pub fn delay_us(us: u64) {
    unsafe {
        let t = rdtsc();
        let n = us * TSC_PER_MS / 1000 + 1;
        while rdtsc().wrapping_sub(t) < n {
            core::hint::spin_loop();
        }
    }
}
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Soak the NC110 agent OS: every 3 min an authenticated ping + stats over bmctl (also keeps the 10-min idle reset
# away, so any reboot seen here is a real event). Log: proof/nc110-soak.log. Stdout = events only (for a Monitor):
# START, REBOOT (boot id changed: unexpected unless we asked), LOST (n consecutive failures), HOURLY summary.
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
LOG=proof/nc110-soak.log; B="tools/.venv/bin/python tools/bmctl.py"
t0=$(date +%s); last_boot=""; fails=0; reboots=0; oks=0; next_hour=$((t0 + 3600))
echo "$(date '+%F %T') START soak" | tee -a "$LOG"
while true; do
  now=$(date +%s)
  if r=$($B stats 2>/dev/null) && p=$($B ping 2>/dev/null); then
    bid=$($B logall 2>/dev/null | grep -o 'boot id [0-9a-f]*' | head -1 | cut -d' ' -f3)
    up=$(sed -n 's/.*uptime_ms=\([0-9]*\).*/\1/p' <<<"$p")
    echo "$(date '+%F %T') ok uptime_ms=$up boot=$bid $r" >> "$LOG"
    [ $fails -ge 2 ] && echo "$(date '+%F %T') RECOVERED after $fails failed checks, uptime_ms=$up" | tee -a "$LOG"
    if [ -n "$last_boot" ] && [ "$bid" != "$last_boot" ]; then
      reboots=$((reboots + 1)); echo "$(date '+%F %T') REBOOT boot id $last_boot -> $bid (uptime_ms=$up)" | tee -a "$LOG"
    fi
    last_boot=$bid; fails=0; oks=$((oks + 1))
  else
    fails=$((fails + 1)); echo "$(date '+%F %T') fail #$fails" >> "$LOG"
    [ $fails = 2 ] && echo "$(date '+%F %T') LOST 2 consecutive checks failed" | tee -a "$LOG"
    [ $fails = 10 ] && echo "$(date '+%F %T') LOST 30 min: may need evnchn (power cycle)" | tee -a "$LOG"
  fi
  if [ "$now" -ge "$next_hour" ]; then
    echo "$(date '+%F %T') HOURLY $(( (now - t0) / 3600 )) h: ok=$oks reboots=$reboots current_boot=$last_boot" | tee -a "$LOG"
    next_hour=$((next_hour + 3600))
  fi
  sleep 180
done

codex
**The watchdog recovers execution, but it does not guarantee recovery of provisioning.** Making the agent OS the persistent default removed the previous escape to Linux after a failed kernel boot.

I reviewed the requested history (two commits exist), generated the entry for `d4275899`, and traced the kernel paths. No edits or live fault injection. The checked-in proof supports successful resets and a missing-kernel fallback; it does not establish recovery from persistent failures.

**MUST-FIX**

1. **Persistent agent-OS failures reboot into themselves indefinitely.**  
   [main.rs:94](~/nc110-baremetal/src/main.rs:94), [installer:53](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/nc110-install-agentos.sh:53)

   | Failure | Current outcome |
   |---|---|
   | GRUB arm returns failure | Attempts to save a one-shot Linux boot, then reboots |
   | Kernel or key file cannot be loaded | Same Linux fallback attempt |
   | Key file loads but its contents are corrupt | Kernel rejects key; resets into the same bad installation |
   | Valid key differs from the operator’s key | bmctl runs but operator cannot authenticate; idle-reset loop |
   | NIC probe/init fails | Waits 60 seconds, resets into the same kernel |
   | DHCP fails | bmctl gives up after 15 seconds; subsequent reset retries the same kernel |
   | bmctl starts but never receives valid commands | Idle reset retries the same kernel |
   | Kernel hangs before its own arming | GRUB’s already-armed watchdog covers it, provided GRUB arming completed |
   | Kernel hangs after arming | Watchdog resets; persistent hang repeats |

   Transient failures can heal. A reproducible kernel/driver/key failure cannot be repaired remotely through the broken channel.

   **Needed:** a bounded boot-failure policy that reaches a known-good recovery environment automatically. Success must mean authenticated provisioning works, not merely that execution reached a petting loop. Preserve a recovery key/image independently of the shared agent key.

2. **A network-loaded kernel can pet forever without provisioning; nothing outside it enforces recovery.**  
   [loader.rs:201](~/nc110-baremetal/src/loader.rs:201)

   The loader checks image integrity, addresses and handoff safety. It cannot establish that the successor implements bmctl or honours the idle deadline. After jumping, the old supervisor is gone. A successor that pets indefinitely while networking is broken defeats the watchdog indefinitely.

   This is a concrete failure mode of privileged experimental kernels, not a hypothetical attacker requirement. **An independently enforced recovery deadline/reset path is needed** for the stated guarantee; another timer implemented inside the replacement kernel remains cooperative.

3. **There is no implemented remote route to Linux for routine persistent repairs.**  
   [bmctl.rs:436](~/nc110-baremetal/src/bmctl.rs:436), [loader.rs:1](~/nc110-baremetal/src/loader.rs:1)

   `reboot` returns to the agent OS. The loader changes RAM only and implements a constrained Multiboot handoff, not a demonstrated Linux boot path. Updating the `/boot` netloader, GRUB configuration, or on-disk key still requires Linux through the supplied installer.

   Shift is explicitly a hands-required route. Even with healthy bmctl, persistent maintenance lacks a supported remote transition. **Add and prove a remote recovery-boot operation**, plus an automatic escape when bmctl itself is unavailable.

4. **The GRUB error fallback depends on an unchecked write succeeding.**  
   [installer:36](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/nc110-install-agentos.sh:36)

   `save_env next_entry nc110_fallback` is followed unconditionally by `sleep` and `reboot`. If saving fails while the old environment remains readable, the next boot selects the failing agent entry again.

   Ext2 is not inherently a problem: GRUB supports its preallocated environment block on plain disks with non-checksumming filesystems through supported firmware disk access. But filesystem type alone does not guarantee writes succeed. The recorded missing-kernel test proves the happy write path only. [GNU GRUB environment-block documentation](https://www.gnu.org/software/grub/manual/grub/html_node/Environment-block.html)

   **Needed:** check persistence failure and provide a same-boot Linux fallback that does not require that write. Separately verify the deployed header’s `next_entry` consumption/clearing behaviour; that header is absent from the reviewed artifacts.

**SHOULD-FIX**

5. **The idle reset is useful, but weaker than “10-minute NIC self-heal.”**  
   [bmctl.rs:262](~/nc110-baremetal/src/bmctl.rs:262), [bmctl.rs:352](~/nc110-baremetal/src/bmctl.rs:352)

   Bounded receive polling and rejecting unauthenticated/replayed traffic as keepalives are good. A nonresponsive NIC normally causes the deadline to expire; a poll that hangs without further pets lets the hardware watchdog fire.

   However:
   - Expiry **returns** to `main`, which logs and pets for another 60 seconds before resetting. Normal recovery is roughly **11 minutes plus boot**, not ten.
   - Fresh authenticated requests refresh `last_ok` before successful reply delivery. A transmit-only failure can remain hidden if an existing client keeps sending fresh authenticated requests.
   - Reset recovery of an actual wedged NIC is not established by the provided proof. The soak log contains only its initial successful sample.

   Test receive-only/transmit-only failures and actual NIC reset recovery. Use an acknowledged round trip if the deadline is intended to represent channel health.

6. **Shift and pre-menu recovery remain unproven boundaries.**  
   [nc110-grubarm-func.cfg:1](~/nc110-baremetal/grub-test/nc110-grubarm-func.cfg:1), [proof:6](~/nc110-baremetal/proof/nc110-agentos-default.md:6)

   Arming occurs inside the selected entry. A cold-boot hang before that point has no protection established by these files. Conversely, if a running TCO survives a reboot, firmware must stop/reload it or reach recovery before expiry. A reset before GRUB reaches its keyboard check cannot be escaped by that check.

   I would **not claim such a loop currently exists**: the recorded successful watchdog reboot argues against it on the tested path. But the full deployed menu/header, Shift behaviour during repeated resets, and Linux’s takeover of an inherited watchdog need verification. Shift alone never satisfies remote recoverability.

**NIT**

- Comments still promise “Slot A on any reset” in `main.rs`, `loader.rs`, and older tooling. Those promises are now false and can mislead an operator during recovery.
- Reset loops are not automatically flash-wear loops: TCO register writes do not write `/boot`. GRUB fallback attempts do repeatedly write the environment block; Linux boots may write logs, and repeated resets impose hardware cycling. Quantitative wear cannot be inferred here. The immediate demonstrated risk is indefinite loss of provisioning.

**Score: 3/10 for “provisioning channel cannot break without needing hands.”**

+## Getting to Slot A Linux (persistent changes: a new agent-OS version, grub.cfg, keys)

+  `watchdog.open_timeout=300` means a Linux that never comes up resets back into the agent OS. The script holds /dev/watchdog
+  once SSH answers. `reboot` from Linux returns to the agent OS.
+- Remote, agent OS unhealthy or unreachable: the crash-loop escape. GRUB counts agent-OS boots per RTC hour, and the 4th goes to
+  stock Slot A Linux (grubenv `nc110_fallback=loop-<hour>`). Three `bmctl reboot`s also get there on purpose.

+## New agent-OS version (from Linux)
+`tools/nc110-install-agentos.sh <kernel>` (new entry, never edits one), test it once with next_entry, then
+`--set-default <sha8>` (grubenv backed up). Rollback = `--set-default <old sha8>`.
+
+## Safety
+- No disk driver in our kernel; /boot is only written from Linux.
+- Any hang leads to a TCO reset, then the agent OS. 10 min without an authenticated command means an idle reset.
+- Persistent failure: crash-loop escape to Linux. Soak: `tools/nc110-soak.sh` writes `proof/nc110-soak.log`.
+- Known limit: a network-loaded kernel that keeps petting the TCO without serving bmctl cannot be stopped from outside.
+  Only kernels from this tree, which always run bmctl with the idle reset, should be loaded. True outside enforcement needs
+  an external power switch (and the battery out).
+

+# Appended once to the NC110 grub.cfg (2026-10-18). Boots stock Slot A Linux in the SAME boot (no grubenv write
+# needed): the end of every failure path in the agent-OS entries (template t3). Same commands as the generated
+# "Alpine NC110, with Linux lts" entry, minus load_video.
+function nc110_linux_now {
+  echo "nc110: booting Slot A Linux now (recovery)"
+  insmod gzio
+  insmod part_msdos
+  insmod ext2
+  search --no-floppy --fs-uuid --set=root c0954bc7-86a0-440c-8401-2c3e95549743
+  linux /vmlinuz-lts root=UUID=b86fb3d7-5127-4800-b36b-b47279ee86ae ro quiet modules=sd-mod,ahci,ata_piix,ext4 rootfstype=ext4 rootdelay=5
+  initrd /initramfs-lts
+  boot
+}

diff --git a/src/boot.s b/src/boot.s
index d7d195e..37d372c 100644
--- a/src/boot.s
+++ b/src/boot.s
@@ -213,11 +213,14 @@ pd:   .skip 4096 * 4

-/* ---- In-RAM chain loader trampoline (bmctl `lcom`). NOT executed here: loader.rs copies the bytes between
-   tramp_start and tramp_end to a free low page and calls it as fn(params). Position-independent (RIP-relative),
-   own GDT and stack inside that page. Order matters: the old kernel's page tables live in its .bss at 1 MiB,
-   which the new image overwrites, so paging is switched OFF before a single byte is copied.
-   params: +0 src, +8 dst, +16 len, +24 bss_len, +32 entry, +40 multiboot info (all u64, < 4 GiB). */
+/* ---- In-RAM chain loader trampoline (bmctl `lcom` / `lcomlinux`). NOT executed here: loader.rs copies the bytes
+   between tramp_start and tramp_end to a free low page and calls it as fn(params). Position-independent (RIP-relative),
+   own GDT and stack inside that page. Order matters: the old kernel's page tables live in its .bss at 1 MiB, which
+   the new image overwrites, so paging is switched OFF before a single byte is copied.
+   params (u64 each, values < 4 GiB): +0 src, +8 dst, +16 len, +24 bss_len, +32 entry, +40 ebx (multiboot info or
+   Linux boot_params), +48 mode (0 = multiboot v1: EAX=0x2BADB002 EBX=info; 1 = Linux 32-bit boot protocol:
+   ESI=boot_params, EBP=EDI=EBX=0). GDT: 0x10 = flat 32-bit code (__BOOT_CS), 0x18 = flat data (__BOOT_DS), as the
+   Linux 32-bit protocol requires; 0x08 = the kernel's 64-bit code so CS stays valid across lgdt. */

+
+const BP: u64 = 0x10000; // Linux boot_params (zero page)
+const BP_CMD: u64 = 0x11000; // Linux command line
+
+/// Linux 32-bit boot protocol (Documentation/arch/x86/boot.rst): protected-mode code goes to code32_start, a zero
+/// page carries the setup header, the command line and an E820 map built from GRUB's multiboot map.
+pub fn prepare_linux(st: &Stage, cmdline: &str) -> Result<Plan, &'static str> {
+    if st.got != st.size {
+        return Err("incomplete image");
+    }
+    let img = unsafe { core::slice::from_raw_parts(STAGE as *const u8, st.size as usize) };
+    let d: [u8; 32] = Sha256::digest(img).into();
+    if d != st.sha {
+        return Err("sha256 mismatch");
+    }
+    if img.len() < 0x1000 || img[0x1fe..0x200] != [0x55, 0xAA] || &img[0x202..0x206] != b"HdrS" {
+        return Err("not a bzImage (boot flag / HdrS)");
+    }
+    let r16 = |o: usize| u16::from_le_bytes([img[o], img[o + 1]]) as u64;
+    let r32 = |o: usize| u32::from_le_bytes(img[o..o + 4].try_into().unwrap()) as u64;
+    let ver = r16(0x206);
+    if ver < 0x020a || img[0x211] & 1 == 0 {
+        return Err("bzImage boot protocol < 2.10 or not LOADED_HIGH");
+    }
+    let setup = if img[0x1f1] == 0 { 4 } else { img[0x1f1] as u64 };
+    let off = (setup + 1) * 512;
+    let (dst, init_size, pref) = (r32(0x214), r32(0x260), r32(0x258) | r32(0x25c) << 32);
+    let len = st.size.checked_sub(off).ok_or("image shorter than its setup")?;
+    if dst != 0x10_0000 || dst + len > STAGE || !mb::ram_contains(dst, len) {
+        return Err("protected-mode code must load at 1 MiB, below staging, in RAM");
+    }
+    let run = pref.max(dst);
+    if pref >= 0x1_0000_0000 || !mb::ram_contains(run, init_size) {
+        return Err("kernel's decompression range (pref_address + init_size) not RAM");
+    }
+    if cmdline.len() > 2047 || !mb::ram_contains(BP, 0x2000) {
+        return Err("cmdline too long, or boot_params pages not RAM");
+    }
+    let tl = (core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_end) as u64) - (core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_start) as u64);
+    if tl > PARAMS - TRAMP || !mb::ram_contains(TRAMP, LOW_END - TRAMP) {
+        return Err("low pages 0x8000-0xA000 not usable");
+    }
+    unsafe {
+        if ORIG_MMAP_TOO_BIG || ORIG_MMAP_LEN == 0 {
+            return Err("no usable GRUB memory map snapshot for the E820 table");
+        }
+        core::ptr::write_bytes(BP as *mut u8, 0, 0x2000);
+        core::ptr::write_bytes(TRAMP as *mut u8, 0, (LOW_END - TRAMP) as usize);
+        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_start), TRAMP as *mut u8, tl as usize);
+        let hend = (0x202 + img[0x201] as usize).min(0x290);
+        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(img.as_ptr().add(0x1f1), (BP + 0x1f1) as *mut u8, hend - 0x1f1);
+        core::ptr::write_volatile((BP + 0x210) as *mut u8, 0xFF); // type_of_loader: undefined
+        core::ptr::write_volatile((BP + 0x228) as *mut u32, BP_CMD as u32); // cmd_line_ptr
+        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(cmdline.as_ptr(), BP_CMD as *mut u8, cmdline.len());
+        // E820 from the multiboot map: entries {size u32, base u64, len u64, type u32}, stride size + 4
+        let mm = &*core::ptr::addr_of!(ORIG_MMAP);
+        let (mut p, mut n) = (0usize, 0u64);
+        while p + 24 <= ORIG_MMAP_LEN && n < 128 {
+            let sz = u32::from_le_bytes(mm[p..p + 4].try_into().unwrap()) as usize;
+            let e = BP + 0x2d0 + 20 * n;
+            core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(mm.as_ptr().add(p + 4), e as *mut u8, 20);
+            n += 1;
+            p += sz + 4;
+        }
+        core::ptr::write_volatile((BP + 0x1e8) as *mut u8, n as u8);
+    }
+    kprintln!("loader: Linux ready: bzImage proto {:#x}, {} B pm code -> {:#x}, init_size {:#x} at {:#x}, cmdline '{}'", ver, len, dst, init_size, run, cmdline);
+    Ok(Plan { params: [STAGE + off, dst, len, 0, dst, BP, 1] })
 }

@@ -0,0 +1,22 @@
+#!/usr/bin/env bash
+# From the agent OS to Slot A Linux with no hands (Codex agent-OS finding 3): chain-load the custom i386 kernel
+# (~/nc110-kbuild, SLOTB-BUILD-FORMULA config + PACKET=y, no initrd, no modules) with root=/dev/sda2.
+# watchdog.open_timeout=300: the kernel feeds the inherited TCO for at most 5 min unless something opens
+# /dev/watchdog, so a Linux that never comes up resets back into the agent OS (the GRUB default) by itself.
+# Once SSH answers this script holds /dev/watchdog open with a 10 s pinger (kill it or `reboot` to go back).
+set -u
+cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
+BZ=${BZ:-$HOME/nc110-kbuild/linux-6.18.44/arch/x86/boot/bzImage}
+S() { ssh -F /dev/null -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=5 -o StrictHostKeyChecking=no -o UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null \
+  -o LogLevel=ERROR -i "$HOME/.ssh/id_ed25519_homelab" root@192.168.50.118 "$@"; }
+tools/.venv/bin/python tools/bmctl.py linux "$BZ" root=/dev/sda2 ro rootfstype=ext4 rootwait console=tty0 watchdog.open_timeout=300 || exit 1
+T0=$(date +%s)
+for _ in $(seq 60); do
+  sleep 5
+  if S 'uname -r; hostname' 2>/dev/null; then
+    S "setsid sh -c 'exec 3>/dev/watchdog; while :; do printf 1 >&3; sleep 10; done' </dev/null >/dev/null 2>&1 &" \
+      && echo "Slot A Linux up after $(( $(date +%s) - T0 )) s; /dev/watchdog held (pinger). 'reboot' returns to the agent OS."
+    exit 0
+  fi
+done
+echo "Linux did not answer SSH within 5 min; the 300 s watchdog open_timeout returns the board to the agent OS"; exit 3

exec
/bin/zsh -lc 'tail -100 proof/codex-review-agentos.txt; git log --oneline e806ac7~1..HEAD; cat proof/nc110-agentos-default.md; cat grub-test/nc110-linux-now.cfg; nl -ba src/loader.rs' in ~/nc110-baremetal
 succeeded in 0ms:

   Bounded receive polling and rejecting unauthenticated/replayed traffic as keepalives are good. A nonresponsive NIC normally causes the deadline to expire; a poll that hangs without further pets lets the hardware watchdog fire.

   However:
   - Expiry **returns** to `main`, which logs and pets for another 60 seconds before resetting. Normal recovery is roughly **11 minutes plus boot**, not ten.
   - Fresh authenticated requests refresh `last_ok` before successful reply delivery. A transmit-only failure can remain hidden if an existing client keeps sending fresh authenticated requests.
   - Reset recovery of an actual wedged NIC is not established by the provided proof. The soak log contains only its initial successful sample.

   Test receive-only/transmit-only failures and actual NIC reset recovery. Use an acknowledged round trip if the deadline is intended to represent channel health.

6. **Shift and pre-menu recovery remain unproven boundaries.**  
   [nc110-grubarm-func.cfg:1](~/nc110-baremetal/grub-test/nc110-grubarm-func.cfg:1), [proof:6](~/nc110-baremetal/proof/nc110-agentos-default.md:6)

   Arming occurs inside the selected entry. A cold-boot hang before that point has no protection established by these files. Conversely, if a running TCO survives a reboot, firmware must stop/reload it or reach recovery before expiry. A reset before GRUB reaches its keyboard check cannot be escaped by that check.

   I would **not claim such a loop currently exists**: the recorded successful watchdog reboot argues against it on the tested path. But the full deployed menu/header, Shift behaviour during repeated resets, and Linux’s takeover of an inherited watchdog need verification. Shift alone never satisfies remote recoverability.

**NIT**

- Comments still promise “Slot A on any reset” in `main.rs`, `loader.rs`, and older tooling. Those promises are now false and can mislead an operator during recovery.
- Reset loops are not automatically flash-wear loops: TCO register writes do not write `/boot`. GRUB fallback attempts do repeatedly write the environment block; Linux boots may write logs, and repeated resets impose hardware cycling. Quantitative wear cannot be inferred here. The immediate demonstrated risk is indefinite loss of provisioning.

**Score: 3/10 for “provisioning channel cannot break without needing hands.”**
tokens used
63,043
**The watchdog recovers execution, but it does not guarantee recovery of provisioning.** Making the agent OS the persistent default removed the previous escape to Linux after a failed kernel boot.

I reviewed the requested history (two commits exist), generated the entry for `d4275899`, and traced the kernel paths. No edits or live fault injection. The checked-in proof supports successful resets and a missing-kernel fallback; it does not establish recovery from persistent failures.

**MUST-FIX**

1. **Persistent agent-OS failures reboot into themselves indefinitely.**  
   [main.rs:94](~/nc110-baremetal/src/main.rs:94), [installer:53](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/nc110-install-agentos.sh:53)

   | Failure | Current outcome |
   |---|---|
   | GRUB arm returns failure | Attempts to save a one-shot Linux boot, then reboots |
   | Kernel or key file cannot be loaded | Same Linux fallback attempt |
   | Key file loads but its contents are corrupt | Kernel rejects key; resets into the same bad installation |
   | Valid key differs from the operator’s key | bmctl runs but operator cannot authenticate; idle-reset loop |
   | NIC probe/init fails | Waits 60 seconds, resets into the same kernel |
   | DHCP fails | bmctl gives up after 15 seconds; subsequent reset retries the same kernel |
   | bmctl starts but never receives valid commands | Idle reset retries the same kernel |
   | Kernel hangs before its own arming | GRUB’s already-armed watchdog covers it, provided GRUB arming completed |
   | Kernel hangs after arming | Watchdog resets; persistent hang repeats |

   Transient failures can heal. A reproducible kernel/driver/key failure cannot be repaired remotely through the broken channel.

   **Needed:** a bounded boot-failure policy that reaches a known-good recovery environment automatically. Success must mean authenticated provisioning works, not merely that execution reached a petting loop. Preserve a recovery key/image independently of the shared agent key.

2. **A network-loaded kernel can pet forever without provisioning; nothing outside it enforces recovery.**  
   [loader.rs:201](~/nc110-baremetal/src/loader.rs:201)

   The loader checks image integrity, addresses and handoff safety. It cannot establish that the successor implements bmctl or honours the idle deadline. After jumping, the old supervisor is gone. A successor that pets indefinitely while networking is broken defeats the watchdog indefinitely.

   This is a concrete failure mode of privileged experimental kernels, not a hypothetical attacker requirement. **An independently enforced recovery deadline/reset path is needed** for the stated guarantee; another timer implemented inside the replacement kernel remains cooperative.

3. **There is no implemented remote route to Linux for routine persistent repairs.**  
   [bmctl.rs:436](~/nc110-baremetal/src/bmctl.rs:436), [loader.rs:1](~/nc110-baremetal/src/loader.rs:1)

   `reboot` returns to the agent OS. The loader changes RAM only and implements a constrained Multiboot handoff, not a demonstrated Linux boot path. Updating the `/boot` netloader, GRUB configuration, or on-disk key still requires Linux through the supplied installer.

   Shift is explicitly a hands-required route. Even with healthy bmctl, persistent maintenance lacks a supported remote transition. **Add and prove a remote recovery-boot operation**, plus an automatic escape when bmctl itself is unavailable.

4. **The GRUB error fallback depends on an unchecked write succeeding.**  
   [installer:36](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/nc110-install-agentos.sh:36)

   `save_env next_entry nc110_fallback` is followed unconditionally by `sleep` and `reboot`. If saving fails while the old environment remains readable, the next boot selects the failing agent entry again.

   Ext2 is not inherently a problem: GRUB supports its preallocated environment block on plain disks with non-checksumming filesystems through supported firmware disk access. But filesystem type alone does not guarantee writes succeed. The recorded missing-kernel test proves the happy write path only. [GNU GRUB environment-block documentation](https://www.gnu.org/software/grub/manual/grub/html_node/Environment-block.html)

   **Needed:** check persistence failure and provide a same-boot Linux fallback that does not require that write. Separately verify the deployed header’s `next_entry` consumption/clearing behaviour; that header is absent from the reviewed artifacts.

**SHOULD-FIX**

5. **The idle reset is useful, but weaker than “10-minute NIC self-heal.”**  
   [bmctl.rs:262](~/nc110-baremetal/src/bmctl.rs:262), [bmctl.rs:352](~/nc110-baremetal/src/bmctl.rs:352)

   Bounded receive polling and rejecting unauthenticated/replayed traffic as keepalives are good. A nonresponsive NIC normally causes the deadline to expire; a poll that hangs without further pets lets the hardware watchdog fire.

   However:
   - Expiry **returns** to `main`, which logs and pets for another 60 seconds before resetting. Normal recovery is roughly **11 minutes plus boot**, not ten.
   - Fresh authenticated requests refresh `last_ok` before successful reply delivery. A transmit-only failure can remain hidden if an existing client keeps sending fresh authenticated requests.
   - Reset recovery of an actual wedged NIC is not established by the provided proof. The soak log contains only its initial successful sample.

   Test receive-only/transmit-only failures and actual NIC reset recovery. Use an acknowledged round trip if the deadline is intended to represent channel health.

6. **Shift and pre-menu recovery remain unproven boundaries.**  
   [nc110-grubarm-func.cfg:1](~/nc110-baremetal/grub-test/nc110-grubarm-func.cfg:1), [proof:6](~/nc110-baremetal/proof/nc110-agentos-default.md:6)

   Arming occurs inside the selected entry. A cold-boot hang before that point has no protection established by these files. Conversely, if a running TCO survives a reboot, firmware must stop/reload it or reach recovery before expiry. A reset before GRUB reaches its keyboard check cannot be escaped by that check.

   I would **not claim such a loop currently exists**: the recorded successful watchdog reboot argues against it on the tested path. But the full deployed menu/header, Shift behaviour during repeated resets, and Linux’s takeover of an inherited watchdog need verification. Shift alone never satisfies remote recoverability.
