# Appended once to the NC110 grub.cfg (2026-10-18). Boots stock Slot A Linux in the SAME boot (no grubenv write
# needed): the end of every failure path in the agent-OS entries (template t3). Same commands as the generated
# "Alpine NC110, with Linux lts" entry, minus load_video.
function nc110_linux_now {
  echo "nc110: booting Slot A Linux now (recovery)"
  insmod gzio
  insmod part_msdos
  insmod ext2
  search --no-floppy --fs-uuid --set=root c0954bc7-86a0-440c-8401-2c3e95549743
  linux /vmlinuz-lts root=UUID=b86fb3d7-5127-4800-b36b-b47279ee86ae ro quiet modules=sd-mod,ahci,ata_piix,ext4 rootfstype=ext4 rootdelay=5
  initrd /initramfs-lts
  boot
}
     1	//! In-RAM chain loader, driven over bmctl (the DS OTA equivalent: nothing is written to /boot).
     2	//! `lbeg <size> <sha256>` opens a staging buffer at STAGE; binary `ldat` ++ u32 offset ++ bytes appends in order;
     3	//! `lcom <cmdline>` verifies length + SHA-256 + the multiboot header (a.out kludge, flag bit 16, as GRUB applies it),
     4	//! checks every destination range, builds a multiboot info block (cmdline, the ORIGINAL GRUB memory map, and a
     5	//! rebuilt 72-byte key module with this boot's seed), quiesces the NIC and jumps through the trampoline in boot.s,
     6	//! which turns paging off, copies the image to load_addr, zeroes its bss and enters it like GRUB would.
     7	//! `lcomlinux <cmdline>` instead boots the staged image as an i386 Linux bzImage (32-bit boot protocol, no initrd):
     8	//! the route from the agent OS back to Slot A Linux without hands (Codex agent-OS review finding 3).
     9	//! The TCO stays armed across the hop (the attended successor re-arms it in kmain). Any refusal leaves us running.
    10	use crate::kprintln;
    11	use crate::mb;
    12	use crate::nic::Rtl;
    13	use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
    14	
    15	pub const STAGE: u64 = 0x0400_0000; // 64 MiB
    16	pub const CAP: u64 = 0x0200_0000; // 32 MiB (bzImage-sized; STAGE + CAP = 96 MiB < 128 MiB, so q35 -m 128 works)
    17	const TRAMP: u64 = 0x8000; // trampoline code + its stack (< 0xC00)
    18	const PARAMS: u64 = 0x8C00;
    19	const INFO: u64 = 0x8D00;
    20	const MODENT: u64 = 0x8E80;
    21	const CMDLINE: u64 = 0x8F00; // <= 255 bytes + NUL
    22	const MODULE: u64 = 0x9000; // page-aligned (our header asks for it: flag bit 0)
    23	const MMAP: u64 = 0x9100;
    24	const LOW_END: u64 = 0xA000;
    25	const MMAP_MAX: usize = (LOW_END - MMAP) as usize;
    26	
    27	/// GRUB's boot metadata, copied out at boot (before any staging could overwrite it). (flags, mem_lower, mem_upper)
    28	static mut ORIG: (u32, u32, u32) = (0, 0, 0);
    29	static mut ORIG_MMAP: [u8; MMAP_MAX] = [0; MMAP_MAX];
    30	static mut ORIG_MMAP_LEN: usize = 0;
    31	static mut ORIG_MMAP_TOO_BIG: bool = false;
    32	
    33	/// Call once, early, with the multiboot info pointer GRUB (or a previous loader) gave us.
    34	pub fn snapshot_boot_info(info: u64) {
    35	    if info == 0 {
    36	        return;
    37	    }
    38	    unsafe {
    39	        let r32 = |a: u64| core::ptr::read_volatile(a as *const u32);
    40	        let f = r32(info);
    41	        ORIG = (f, if f & 1 != 0 { r32(info + 4) } else { 0 }, if f & 1 != 0 { r32(info + 8) } else { 0 });
    42	        if f & (1 << 6) != 0 {
    43	            let (l, a) = (r32(info + 44) as usize, r32(info + 48) as u64);
    44	            if l <= MMAP_MAX {
    45	                core::ptr::copy(a as *const u8, core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(ORIG_MMAP) as *mut u8, l);
    46	                ORIG_MMAP_LEN = l;
    47	            } else {
    48	                ORIG_MMAP_TOO_BIG = true;
    49	            }
    50	        }
    51	    }
    52	}
    53	
    54	extern "C" {
    55	    static tramp_start: u8;
    56	    static tramp_end: u8;
    57	    static __load_start: u8;
    58	    static __bss_end: u8;
    59	}
    60	
    61	pub struct Stage {
    62	    size: u64,
    63	    sha: [u8; 32],
    64	    got: u64,
    65	}
    66	
    67	fn overlaps(a: u64, al: u64, b: u64, bl: u64) -> bool {
    68	    a < b.saturating_add(bl) && b < a.saturating_add(al)
    69	}
    70	
    71	fn kernel_image() -> (u64, u64) {
    72	    let (s, e) = (core::ptr::addr_of!(__load_start) as u64, core::ptr::addr_of!(__bss_end) as u64);
    73	    (s, e - s)
    74	}
    75	
    76	pub fn begin(size: u64, sha_hex: &str) -> Result<Stage, &'static str> {
    77	    if size == 0 || size > CAP {
    78	        return Err("size 1..32 MiB");
    79	    }
    80	    let (ks, kl) = kernel_image();
    81	    if !mb::ram_contains(STAGE, size) || overlaps(STAGE, size, ks, kl) {
    82	        return Err("staging area not free RAM");
    83	    }
    84	    if sha_hex.len() != 64 || !sha_hex.bytes().all(|c| c.is_ascii_hexdigit()) {
    85	        return Err("sha256: 64 hex chars");
    86	    }
    87	    let mut sha = [0u8; 32];
    88	    for (i, b) in sha.iter_mut().enumerate() {
    89	        *b = u8::from_str_radix(&sha_hex[2 * i..2 * i + 2], 16).map_err(|_| "sha256: hex")?;
    90	    }
    91	    Ok(Stage { size, sha, got: 0 })
    92	}
    93	
    94	pub fn data(st: &mut Stage, off: u64, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<u64, &'static str> {
    95	    if off != st.got {
    96	        return Err("out of order (send chunks in sequence)");
    97	    }
    98	    let end = off.checked_add(bytes.len() as u64).ok_or("overflow")?;
    99	    if end > st.size {
   100	        return Err("past announced size");
   101	    }
   102	    unsafe { core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(bytes.as_ptr(), (STAGE + off) as *mut u8, bytes.len()) };
   103	    st.got = end;
   104	    Ok(end)
   105	}
   106	
   107	/// Everything checked, nothing moved yet. Returns (src, dst, len, bss_len, entry).
   108	fn plan(st: &Stage) -> Result<(u64, u64, u64, u64, u64), &'static str> {
   109	    if st.got != st.size {
   110	        return Err("incomplete image");
   111	    }
   112	    let img = unsafe { core::slice::from_raw_parts(STAGE as *const u8, st.size as usize) };
   113	    let d: [u8; 32] = Sha256::digest(img).into();
   114	    if d != st.sha {
   115	        return Err("sha256 mismatch");
   116	    }
   117	    let rd = |o: usize| u32::from_le_bytes(img[o..o + 4].try_into().unwrap());
   118	    let lim = img.len().min(8192);
   119	    let hoff = (0..lim.saturating_sub(31)).step_by(4).find(|&o| {
   120	        rd(o) == 0x1BAD_B002 && rd(o).wrapping_add(rd(o + 4)).wrapping_add(rd(o + 8)) == 0
   121	    }).ok_or("no multiboot header in the first 8 KiB")?;
   122	    let flags = rd(hoff + 4);
   123	    if flags & (1 << 16) == 0 {
   124	        return Err("multiboot header lacks the address kludge (flag 16)");
   125	    }
   126	    if flags & 0xFFFC != 0 {
   127	        return Err("multiboot header requires features this loader does not provide (bits 2..15)");
   128	    }
   129	    let (haddr, laddr, lend, bend, entry) =
   130	        (rd(hoff + 12) as u64, rd(hoff + 16) as u64, rd(hoff + 20) as u64, rd(hoff + 24) as u64, rd(hoff + 28) as u64);
   131	    if laddr > haddr || lend <= laddr || bend < lend || entry < laddr || entry >= lend {
   132	        return Err("inconsistent header addresses");
   133	    }
   134	    let foff = (hoff as u64).checked_sub(haddr - laddr).ok_or("header offset")?;
   135	    let len = lend - laddr;
   136	    if foff.checked_add(len).is_none_or(|e| e > st.size) {
   137	        return Err("image shorter than load_end - load_addr");
   138	    }
   139	    if laddr < 0x10_0000 || bend > STAGE || !mb::ram_contains(laddr, bend - laddr) {
   140	        return Err("target range not RAM in [1 MiB, staging)");
   141	    }
   142	    if overlaps(laddr, bend - laddr, TRAMP, LOW_END - TRAMP) {
   143	        return Err("target overlaps the trampoline/info pages");
   144	    }
   145	    Ok((STAGE + foff, laddr, len, bend - lend, entry))
   146	}
   147	
   148	pub struct Plan {
   149	    params: [u64; 7], // src, dst, len, bss, entry, ebx (info / boot_params), mode (0 multiboot, 1 Linux)
   150	}
   151	
   152	/// All checks plus writing the low pages (trampoline, info, module, cmdline, mmap). Nothing of ours is touched:
   153	/// the low pages are outside the kernel image; GRUB's info/mmap come from the boot-time snapshot.
   154	pub fn prepare(st: &Stage, cmdline: &str, seed: &[u8; 32]) -> Result<Plan, &'static str> {
   155	    let (src, dst, len, bss, entry) = plan(st)?;
   156	    let tl = (core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_end) as u64) - (core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_start) as u64);
   157	    if tl > PARAMS - TRAMP || !mb::ram_contains(TRAMP, LOW_END - TRAMP) {
   158	        return Err("low pages 0x8000-0xA000 not usable");
   159	    }
   160	    if cmdline.len() > 255 {
   161	        return Err("cmdline > 255");
   162	    }
   163	    unsafe {
   164	        if ORIG_MMAP_TOO_BIG {
   165	            return Err("GRUB memory map larger than the loader's buffer: refusing rather than dropping it");
   166	        }
   167	        let (oflags, mlo, mhi) = ORIG;
   168	        let ml = ORIG_MMAP_LEN;
   169	        let mmap = &*core::ptr::addr_of!(ORIG_MMAP);
   170	        let w32 = |a: u64, v: u32| core::ptr::write_volatile(a as *mut u32, v);
   171	        core::ptr::write_bytes(TRAMP as *mut u8, 0, (LOW_END - TRAMP) as usize);
   172	        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_start), TRAMP as *mut u8, tl as usize);
   173	        // key module: MAGIC + seed + public key, exactly the 72 bytes GRUB's `module` line gave us
   174	        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(mb::MAGIC.as_ptr(), MODULE as *mut u8, 8);
   175	        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(seed.as_ptr(), (MODULE + 8) as *mut u8, 32);
   176	        let publ = sunset::ed25519_dalek::SigningKey::from_bytes(seed).verifying_key().to_bytes();
   177	        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(publ.as_ptr(), (MODULE + 40) as *mut u8, 32);
   178	        w32(MODENT, MODULE as u32);
   179	        w32(MODENT + 4, (MODULE + 72) as u32);
   180	        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(cmdline.as_ptr(), CMDLINE as *mut u8, cmdline.len());
   181	        // multiboot info: flags 0 (mem), 2 (cmdline), 3 (mods), 6 (GRUB's memory map, verbatim)
   182	        let mut flags = (1 << 2) | (1 << 3);
   183	        if oflags & 1 != 0 {
   184	            flags |= 1;
   185	            w32(INFO + 4, mlo);
   186	            w32(INFO + 8, mhi);
   187	        }
   188	        if ml > 0 {
   189	            core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(mmap.as_ptr(), MMAP as *mut u8, ml);
   190	            flags |= 1 << 6;
   191	            w32(INFO + 44, ml as u32);
   192	            w32(INFO + 48, MMAP as u32);
   193	        }
   194	        w32(INFO, flags);
   195	        w32(INFO + 16, CMDLINE as u32);
   196	        w32(INFO + 20, 1);
   197	        w32(INFO + 24, MODENT as u32);
   198	    }
   199	    kprintln!("loader: ready: {} B image -> {:#x}..{:#x} (+{} B bss), entry {:#x}, cmdline '{}'", st.size, dst, dst + len, bss, entry, cmdline);
   200	    Ok(Plan { params: [src, dst, len, bss, entry, INFO, 0] })
   201	}
   202	
   203	const BP: u64 = 0x10000; // Linux boot_params (zero page)
   204	const BP_CMD: u64 = 0x11000; // Linux command line
   205	
   206	/// Linux 32-bit boot protocol (Documentation/arch/x86/boot.rst): protected-mode code goes to code32_start, a zero
   207	/// page carries the setup header, the command line and an E820 map built from GRUB's multiboot map.
   208	pub fn prepare_linux(st: &Stage, cmdline: &str) -> Result<Plan, &'static str> {
   209	    if st.got != st.size {
   210	        return Err("incomplete image");
   211	    }
   212	    let img = unsafe { core::slice::from_raw_parts(STAGE as *const u8, st.size as usize) };
   213	    let d: [u8; 32] = Sha256::digest(img).into();
   214	    if d != st.sha {
   215	        return Err("sha256 mismatch");
   216	    }
   217	    if img.len() < 0x1000 || img[0x1fe..0x200] != [0x55, 0xAA] || &img[0x202..0x206] != b"HdrS" {
   218	        return Err("not a bzImage (boot flag / HdrS)");
   219	    }
   220	    let r16 = |o: usize| u16::from_le_bytes([img[o], img[o + 1]]) as u64;
   221	    let r32 = |o: usize| u32::from_le_bytes(img[o..o + 4].try_into().unwrap()) as u64;
   222	    let ver = r16(0x206);
   223	    if ver < 0x020a || img[0x211] & 1 == 0 {
   224	        return Err("bzImage boot protocol < 2.10 or not LOADED_HIGH");
   225	    }
   226	    let setup = if img[0x1f1] == 0 { 4 } else { img[0x1f1] as u64 };
   227	    let off = (setup + 1) * 512;
   228	    let (dst, init_size, pref) = (r32(0x214), r32(0x260), r32(0x258) | r32(0x25c) << 32);
   229	    let len = st.size.checked_sub(off).ok_or("image shorter than its setup")?;
   230	    if dst != 0x10_0000 || dst + len > STAGE || !mb::ram_contains(dst, len) {
   231	        return Err("protected-mode code must load at 1 MiB, below staging, in RAM");
   232	    }
   233	    let run = pref.max(dst);
   234	    if pref >= 0x1_0000_0000 || !mb::ram_contains(run, init_size) {
   235	        return Err("kernel's decompression range (pref_address + init_size) not RAM");
   236	    }
   237	    if cmdline.len() > 2047 || !mb::ram_contains(BP, 0x2000) {
   238	        return Err("cmdline too long, or boot_params pages not RAM");
   239	    }
   240	    let tl = (core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_end) as u64) - (core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_start) as u64);
   241	    if tl > PARAMS - TRAMP || !mb::ram_contains(TRAMP, LOW_END - TRAMP) {
   242	        return Err("low pages 0x8000-0xA000 not usable");
   243	    }
   244	    unsafe {
   245	        if ORIG_MMAP_TOO_BIG || ORIG_MMAP_LEN == 0 {
   246	            return Err("no usable GRUB memory map snapshot for the E820 table");
   247	        }
   248	        core::ptr::write_bytes(BP as *mut u8, 0, 0x2000);
   249	        core::ptr::write_bytes(TRAMP as *mut u8, 0, (LOW_END - TRAMP) as usize);
   250	        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_start), TRAMP as *mut u8, tl as usize);
   251	        let hend = (0x202 + img[0x201] as usize).min(0x290);
   252	        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(img.as_ptr().add(0x1f1), (BP + 0x1f1) as *mut u8, hend - 0x1f1);
   253	        core::ptr::write_volatile((BP + 0x210) as *mut u8, 0xFF); // type_of_loader: undefined
   254	        core::ptr::write_volatile((BP + 0x228) as *mut u32, BP_CMD as u32); // cmd_line_ptr
   255	        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(cmdline.as_ptr(), BP_CMD as *mut u8, cmdline.len());
   256	        // E820 from the multiboot map: entries {size u32, base u64, len u64, type u32}, stride size + 4
   257	        let mm = &*core::ptr::addr_of!(ORIG_MMAP);
   258	        let (mut p, mut n) = (0usize, 0u64);
   259	        while p + 24 <= ORIG_MMAP_LEN && n < 128 {
   260	            let sz = u32::from_le_bytes(mm[p..p + 4].try_into().unwrap()) as usize;
   261	            let e = BP + 0x2d0 + 20 * n;
   262	            core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(mm.as_ptr().add(p + 4), e as *mut u8, 20);
   263	            n += 1;
   264	            p += sz + 4;
   265	        }
   266	        core::ptr::write_volatile((BP + 0x1e8) as *mut u8, n as u8);
   267	    }
   268	    kprintln!("loader: Linux ready: bzImage proto {:#x}, {} B pm code -> {:#x}, init_size {:#x} at {:#x}, cmdline '{}'", ver, len, dst, init_size, run, cmdline);
   269	    Ok(Plan { params: [STAGE + off, dst, len, 0, dst, BP, 1] })
   270	}
   271	
   272	/// Point of no return: NIC quiesced (reset completed), trampoline entered. The TCO stays armed across the hop.
   273	/// If the NIC reset does not complete, its DMA may still target memory the new image would overwrite: hard reset
   274	/// instead of jumping (GRUB default, the agent OS, then the crash-loop escape).
   275	pub fn jump(p: &Plan, nic: &Rtl) -> ! {
   276	    if let Err(e) = nic.quiesce() {
   277	        kprintln!("loader: NIC quiesce failed ({}): NOT jumping, hard reset", e);
   278	        crate::wdt::hard_reset();
   279	    }
   280	    unsafe {
   281	        for (i, v) in p.params.iter().enumerate() {
   282	            core::ptr::write_volatile((PARAMS + 8 * i as u64) as *mut u64, *v);
   283	        }
   284	        crate::wdt::pet();
   285	        let f: extern "sysv64" fn(u64) -> ! = core::mem::transmute(TRAMP as usize);
   286	        f(PARAMS)
   287	    }
   288	}

   216	/* ---- In-RAM chain loader trampoline (bmctl `lcom` / `lcomlinux`). NOT executed here: loader.rs copies the bytes
   217	   between tramp_start and tramp_end to a free low page and calls it as fn(params). Position-independent (RIP-relative),
   218	   own GDT and stack inside that page. Order matters: the old kernel's page tables live in its .bss at 1 MiB, which

//! Multiboot v1 info: cmdline, memory map, and the client-key module (M2.5).
//!
//! The SSH client key is NOT compiled into the ELF. GRUB loads it with `module /baremetal/<file>` (QEMU:
//! `-initrd <file>`), so the key lives on /boot and can be rotated without a rebuild.
//! Module format (72 bytes): b"NC110K1\n" | ed25519 seed (32) | ed25519 public key (32).
//! The kernel derives the public key from the seed and refuses on mismatch (truncation / corruption / wrong
//! file), copies the seed into a static, then zeroes the module bytes in RAM.
use crate::kprintln;
use core::ptr::{read_volatile, write_volatile};

pub const MAGIC: &[u8; 8] = b"NC110K1\n";
const KEYMOD_LEN: u64 = 72;

static mut RAM: [(u64, u64); 32] = [(0, 0); 32];
static mut NRAM: usize = 0;
static mut HAVE_MAP: bool = false;

unsafe fn rd32(a: u64) -> u32 {
    read_volatile(a as *const u32)
}

/// Record available-RAM ranges (mmap type 1, or mem_lower/mem_upper as a fallback).
pub fn parse_memory(info: u64) {
    unsafe {
        if info == 0 {
            return;
        }
        let flags = rd32(info);
        if flags & (1 << 6) != 0 {
            let len = rd32(info + 44) as u64;
            let mut p = rd32(info + 48) as u64;
            let end = p + len;
            while p + 24 <= end && NRAM < 32 {
                let size = rd32(p) as u64;
                let base = read_volatile((p + 4) as *const u64);
                let l = read_volatile((p + 12) as *const u64);
                let ty = rd32(p + 20);
                if ty == 1 && l > 0 {
                    RAM[NRAM] = (base, l);
                    NRAM += 1;
                }
                p += size + 4;
            }
            HAVE_MAP = true;
        } else if flags & 1 != 0 {
            RAM[0] = (0, rd32(info + 4) as u64 * 1024);
            RAM[1] = (0x10_0000, rd32(info + 8) as u64 * 1024);
            NRAM = 2;
            HAVE_MAP = true;
        }
    }
}

/// True if [base, base+len) touches available RAM, or if no memory map was given (fail closed).
pub fn ram_overlaps(base: u64, len: u64) -> bool {
    unsafe {
        if !HAVE_MAP {
            return true;
        }
        let ram = &*core::ptr::addr_of!(RAM);
        ram[..NRAM].iter().any(|&(b, l)| base < b + l && b < base + len)
    }
}

/// True only if [base, base+len) lies entirely inside ONE available-RAM range of the multiboot map.
pub fn ram_contains(base: u64, len: u64) -> bool {
    let Some(end) = base.checked_add(len) else { return false };
    unsafe {
        let ram = &*core::ptr::addr_of!(RAM);
        HAVE_MAP && ram[..NRAM].iter().any(|&(b, l)| base >= b && end <= b.saturating_add(l))
    }
}

pub fn cmdline(info: u64) -> &'static str {
    unsafe {
        if info == 0 || rd32(info) & 4 == 0 {
            return "";
        }
        let p = rd32(info + 16) as *const u8;
        let mut n = 0;
        while n < 256 && *p.add(n) != 0 {
            n += 1;
        }
        core::str::from_utf8(core::slice::from_raw_parts(p, n)).unwrap_or("")
    }
}

/// Find the key module, validate it, copy the seed out, wipe the module. None (with a log line) if absent/bad.
pub fn take_client_key(info: u64) -> Option<[u8; 32]> {
    unsafe {
        if info == 0 || rd32(info) & (1 << 3) == 0 || rd32(info + 20) == 0 {
            kprintln!("keymod: no multiboot module");
            return None;
        }
        let count = rd32(info + 20).min(16) as u64;
        let mods = rd32(info + 24) as u64;
        for i in 0..count {
            let e = mods + 16 * i;
            let (start, end) = (rd32(e) as u64, rd32(e + 4) as u64);
            if end <= start || end - start != KEYMOD_LEN {
                continue;
            }
            let m = core::slice::from_raw_parts(start as *const u8, KEYMOD_LEN as usize);
            if &m[..8] != MAGIC {
                continue;
            }
            let mut seed = [0u8; 32];
            let mut publ = [0u8; 32];
            seed.copy_from_slice(&m[8..40]);
            publ.copy_from_slice(&m[40..72]);
            for j in 0..KEYMOD_LEN {
                write_volatile((start + j) as *mut u8, 0);
            }
            let derived = sunset::ed25519_dalek::SigningKey::from_bytes(&seed).verifying_key().to_bytes();
            if derived != publ {
                kprintln!("keymod: module {} public key does not match its seed -> ignored (wiped)", i);
                return None;
            }
            kprintln!("keymod: client key from module {} at {:#x} (72 B), public key verified, module wiped", i, start);
            return Some(seed);
        }
        kprintln!("keymod: {} module(s), none is a 72-byte NC110K1 key", count);
        None
    }
}

+## Getting to Slot A Linux (persistent changes: a new agent-OS version, grub.cfg, keys)

+  stock Slot A Linux (grubenv `nc110_fallback=loop-<hour>`). Three `bmctl reboot`s also get there on purpose.

+## New agent-OS version (from Linux)
+`tools/nc110-install-agentos.sh <kernel>` (new entry, never edits one), test it once with next_entry, then
+`--set-default <sha8>` (grubenv backed up). Rollback = `--set-default <old sha8>`.
+
+## Safety
+- No disk driver in our kernel; /boot is only written from Linux.
+- Any hang leads to a TCO reset, then the agent OS. 10 min without an authenticated command means an idle reset.
+- Persistent failure: crash-loop escape to Linux. Soak: `tools/nc110-soak.sh` writes `proof/nc110-soak.log`.
+- Known limit: a network-loaded kernel that keeps petting the TCO without serving bmctl cannot be stopped from outside.
+  Only kernels from this tree, which always run bmctl with the idle reset, should be loaded. True outside enforcement needs
+  an external power switch (and the battery out).

Round 2 establishes the remote Linux route and the fourth-boot escape. The remaining recovery gaps are slower failures that never reach four boots in an RTC hour, and watchdog petting that continues after provisioning fails—including the new Linux pinger. I’m checking the loader’s range validation separately from the successful boot proof.

/*
 * These symbols needed to be marked as .hidden to prevent the BFD linker from
 * generating R_386_32 (rather than R_386_RELATIVE) relocations for them when
 * the 32-bit compressed kernel is linked as PIE. This is no longer necessary,
 * but it doesn't hurt to keep them .hidden.
 */
	.hidden _bss
	.hidden _ebss
	.hidden _end

	__HEAD
SYM_FUNC_START(startup_32)
	cld
	cli

/*
 * Calculate the delta between where we were compiled to run
 * at and where we were actually loaded at.  This can only be done
 * with a short local call on x86.  Nothing  else will tell us what
 * address we are running at.  The reserved chunk of the real-mode
 * data at 0x1e4 (defined as a scratch field) are used as the stack
 * for this calculation. Only 4 bytes are needed.
 */
	leal	(BP_scratch+4)(%esi), %esp
	call	1f
1:	popl	%edx
	addl	$_GLOBAL_OFFSET_TABLE_+(.-1b), %edx

	/* Load new GDT */
	leal	gdt@GOTOFF(%edx), %eax
	movl	%eax, 2(%eax)
	lgdt	(%eax)

	/* Load segment registers with our descriptors */
	movl	$__BOOT_DS, %eax
	movl	%eax, %ds
	movl	%eax, %es
	movl	%eax, %fs
	movl	%eax, %gs
	movl	%eax, %ss

/*
 * %edx contains the address we are loaded at by the boot loader (plus the
 * offset to the GOT).  The below code calculates %ebx to be the address where
 * we should move the kernel image temporarily for safe in-place decompression
 * (again, plus the offset to the GOT).
 *
 * %ebp is calculated to be the address that the kernel will be decompressed to.
 */

#ifdef CONFIG_RELOCATABLE
	leal	startup_32@GOTOFF(%edx), %ebx
	movl	BP_kernel_alignment(%esi), %eax
	decl	%eax
	addl    %eax, %ebx
	notl	%eax
	andl    %eax, %ebx
	cmpl	$LOAD_PHYSICAL_ADDR, %ebx
	jae	1f
#endif
	movl	$LOAD_PHYSICAL_ADDR, %ebx
1:

	movl	%ebx, %ebp	// Save the output address for later
	/* Target address to relocate to for decompression */
	addl    BP_init_size(%esi), %ebx
	subl    $_end@GOTOFF, %ebx

	/* Set up the stack */
	leal	boot_stack_end@GOTOFF(%ebx), %esp

	/* Zero EFLAGS */
	pushl	$0
	popfl

/*
 * Copy the compressed kernel to the end of our buffer
 * where decompression in place becomes safe.
 */
	pushl	%esi
	leal	(_bss@GOTOFF-4)(%edx), %esi
	leal	(_bss@GOTOFF-4)(%ebx), %edi
	movl	$(_bss - startup_32), %ecx
	shrl	$2, %ecx
	std
	rep	movsl
	cld
	popl	%esi

	/*
	 * The GDT may get overwritten either during the copy we just did or
	 * during extract_kernel below. To avoid any issues, repoint the GDTR
	 * to the new copy of the GDT.
	 */
	leal	gdt@GOTOFF(%ebx), %eax
	movl	%eax, 2(%eax)
	lgdt	(%eax)

/*
 * Jump to the relocated address.
 */
	leal	.Lrelocated@GOTOFF(%ebx), %eax
	jmp	*%eax
SYM_FUNC_END(startup_32)

	.text
SYM_FUNC_START_LOCAL_NOALIGN(.Lrelocated)

/*
 * Clear BSS (stack is currently empty)
 */
	xorl	%eax, %eax
	leal	_bss@GOTOFF(%ebx), %edi
	leal	_ebss@GOTOFF(%ebx), %ecx
	subl	%edi, %ecx
	shrl	$2, %ecx
870:Field name:	pref_address
871-Type:		read (reloc)
872-Offset/size:	0x258/8
873-Protocol:	2.10+
874-============	============
875-
876-  This field, if nonzero, represents a preferred load address for the
877-  kernel.  A relocating bootloader should attempt to load at this
878-  address if possible.
879-
880-  A non-relocatable kernel will unconditionally move itself and to run
881-  at this address. A relocatable kernel will move itself to this address if it
882-  loaded below this address.
883-
884-============	=======
885:Field name:	init_size
886-Type:		read
887-Offset/size:	0x260/4
888-============	=======
889-
890-  This field indicates the amount of linear contiguous memory starting
891-  at the kernel runtime start address that the kernel needs before it
892-  is capable of examining its memory map.  This is not the same thing
893-  as the total amount of memory the kernel needs to boot, but it can
894-  be used by a relocating boot loader to help select a safe load
895-  address for the kernel.
896-
897-  The kernel runtime start address is determined by the following algorithm::
898-
899-   if (relocatable_kernel) {
900-    	if (load_address < pref_address)
901-    		load_address = pref_address;
902-    	runtime_start = align_up(load_address, kernel_alignment);
903-   } else {
904-    	runtime_start = pref_address;
905-   }
906-
907-Hence the necessary memory window location and size can be estimated by
908-a boot loader as::
909-
910-   memory_window_start = runtime_start;
911-   memory_window_size = init_size;
912-
913-============	===============
914-Field name:	handover_offset
915-Type:		read
916-Offset/size:	0x264/4
917-============	===============
918-
919-  This field is the offset from the beginning of the kernel image to
920-  the EFI handover protocol entry point. Boot loaders using the EFI
921-  handover protocol to boot the kernel should jump to this offset.
922-
923-  See EFI HANDOVER PROTOCOL below for more details.
924-

931-
932-  This field is the offset from the beginning of the kernel image to the
933-  kernel_info. The kernel_info structure is embedded in the Linux image

1324:32-bit Boot Protocol
1325-====================
1326-
1327-For machine with some new BIOS other than legacy BIOS, such as EFI,
1328-LinuxBIOS, etc, and kexec, the 16-bit real mode setup code in kernel
1329-based on legacy BIOS can not be used, so a 32-bit boot protocol needs
1330-to be defined.
1331-
1332-In 32-bit boot protocol, the first step in loading a Linux kernel
1333-should be to setup the boot parameters (struct boot_params,
1334-traditionally known as "zero page"). The memory for struct boot_params
1335-should be allocated and initialized to all zero. Then the setup header
1336-from offset 0x01f1 of kernel image on should be loaded into struct
1337-boot_params and examined. The end of setup header can be calculated as
1338-follow::
1339-
1340-  0x0202 + byte value at offset 0x0201
1341-
1342-In addition to read/modify/write the setup header of the struct
1343-boot_params as that of 16-bit boot protocol, the boot loader should
1344-also fill the additional fields of the struct boot_params as
1345-described in chapter Documentation/arch/x86/zero-page.rst.
1346-
1347-After setting up the struct boot_params, the boot loader can load the
1348-32/64-bit kernel in the same way as that of 16-bit boot protocol.
1349-
1350-In 32-bit boot protocol, the kernel is started by jumping to the
1351-32-bit kernel entry point, which is the start address of loaded
1352-32/64-bit kernel.
1353-
1354-At entry, the CPU must be in 32-bit protected mode with paging
1355-disabled; a GDT must be loaded with the descriptors for selectors
1356-__BOOT_CS(0x10) and __BOOT_DS(0x18); both descriptors must be 4G flat
1357-segment; __BOOT_CS must have execute/read permission, and __BOOT_DS
1358-must have read/write permission; CS must be __BOOT_CS and DS, ES, SS
1359-must be __BOOT_DS; interrupt must be disabled; %esi must hold the base
1360-address of the struct boot_params; %ebp, %edi and %ebx must be zero.
1361-
1362-64-bit Boot Protocol
1363-====================
1364-
1365-For machine with 64bit cpus and 64bit kernel, we could use 64bit bootloader
1366-and we need a 64-bit boot protocol.
1367-
1368-In 64-bit boot protocol, the first step in loading a Linux kernel
1369-should be to setup the boot parameters (struct boot_params,
1370-traditionally known as "zero page"). The memory for struct boot_params
1371-could be allocated anywhere (even above 4G) and initialized to all zero.
1372-Then, the setup header at offset 0x01f1 of kernel image on should be
1373-loaded into struct boot_params and examined. The end of setup header
1374-can be calculated as follows::
1375-
1376-  0x0202 + byte value at offset 0x0201
1377-
1378-In addition to read/modify/write the setup header of the struct
1379-boot_params as that of 16-bit boot protocol, the boot loader should
1380-also fill the additional fields of the struct boot_params as described
1381-in chapter Documentation/arch/x86/zero-page.rst.
1382-
1383-After setting up the struct boot_params, the boot loader can load
1384-64-bit kernel in the same way as that of 16-bit boot protocol, but
1385-kernel could be loaded above 4G.
1386-
1387-In 64-bit boot protocol, the kernel is started by jumping to the
1388-64-bit kernel entry point, which is the start address of loaded
1389-64-bit kernel plus 0x200.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Soak the NC110 agent OS: every 3 min an authenticated ping + stats over bmctl (also keeps the 10-min idle reset
# away, so any reboot seen here is a real event). Log: proof/nc110-soak.log. Stdout = events only (for a Monitor):
# START, REBOOT (boot id changed: unexpected unless we asked), LOST (n consecutive failures), HOURLY summary.
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
LOG=proof/nc110-soak.log; B="tools/.venv/bin/python tools/bmctl.py"
t0=$(date +%s); last_boot=""; fails=0; reboots=0; oks=0; next_hour=$((t0 + 3600))
echo "$(date '+%F %T') START soak" | tee -a "$LOG"
while true; do
  now=$(date +%s)
  if r=$($B stats 2>/dev/null) && p=$($B ping 2>/dev/null); then
    bid=$($B logall 2>/dev/null | grep -o 'boot id [0-9a-f]*' | head -1 | cut -d' ' -f3)
    up=$(sed -n 's/.*uptime_ms=\([0-9]*\).*/\1/p' <<<"$p")
    echo "$(date '+%F %T') ok uptime_ms=$up boot=$bid $r" >> "$LOG"
    [ $fails -ge 2 ] && echo "$(date '+%F %T') RECOVERED after $fails failed checks, uptime_ms=$up" | tee -a "$LOG"
    if [ -n "$last_boot" ] && [ "$bid" != "$last_boot" ]; then
      reboots=$((reboots + 1)); echo "$(date '+%F %T') REBOOT boot id $last_boot -> $bid (uptime_ms=$up)" | tee -a "$LOG"
    fi
    last_boot=$bid; fails=0; oks=$((oks + 1))
  else
    fails=$((fails + 1)); echo "$(date '+%F %T') fail #$fails" >> "$LOG"
    [ $fails = 2 ] && echo "$(date '+%F %T') LOST 2 consecutive checks failed" | tee -a "$LOG"
    [ $fails = 10 ] && echo "$(date '+%F %T') LOST 30 min: may need evnchn (power cycle)" | tee -a "$LOG"
  fi
  if [ "$now" -ge "$next_hour" ]; then
    echo "$(date '+%F %T') HOURLY $(( (now - t0) / 3600 )) h: ok=$oks reboots=$reboots current_boot=$last_boot" | tee -a "$LOG"
    next_hour=$((next_hour + 3600))
  fi
  sleep 180
done

codex
**Material improvement: findings 3 and 4 are fixed for the demonstrated path; finding 1 is substantially reduced.** Reviewed both commits and round-2 evidence; no edits or live fault injection.

| Original finding | Reassessment |
|---|---|
| **1. Persistent failures loop forever** | **Partially fixed / SHOULD-FIX.** Fast failures and default ~11-minute idle cycles now escape. Four boots within one RTC hour is not a general bounded-failure policy. |
| **2. Successor pets forever without bmctl** | **MUST-FIX, unchanged.** No independent recovery enforcement. |
| **3. No remote Linux maintenance route** | **Closed for the tested custom bzImage.** Hardware proof establishes agent OS → Slot A SSH. Loader hardening remains below. |
| **4. Failed `save_env` causes reboot loop** | **Closed for returned write errors with working Linux fallback.** Recovery no longer depends on successfully saving the breadcrumb. |
| **5. Idle reset overclaims channel health** | **SHOULD-FIX, unchanged.** Approximately 11 minutes; fresh authenticated requests refresh the deadline before reply delivery. Soak evidence improved, but does not demonstrate NIC fault recovery. |
| **6. Pre-GRUB-arm hangs** | **SHOULD-FIX, unchanged.** No established remote recovery before arming; successful normal resets do not cover this boundary. |

**MUST-FIX**

- **The Linux route introduces another indefinite petting case.** [nc110-to-linux.sh:16](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/nc110-to-linux.sh:16) starts a detached, unconditional watchdog pinger after one successful SSH connection. Subsequent NIC/SSH failure leaves Linux alive and petting forever. `open_timeout=300` protects the period before first open; it is not a continuing provisioning deadline. Require an expiring externally renewed lease or independent reset control.

**SHOULD-FIX**

- **Counter semantics need tightening.** [installer:20](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/nc110-install-agentos.sh:20): hour rollover discards accumulated failures. A repeatable failure producing a reboot every 21 minutes can run forever without four boots in any hour. Stable empty date variables produce `"---"` and still escape on boot four; intermittent valid/empty values or RTC jumps repeatedly clear the count. Exact equality with `"xxxx"` also fails open for malformed/overlong counter values. Prefer validated state and a failure budget cleared by confirmed provisioning health.

- **Fallback is an attempt, not a terminal recovery guarantee.** [installer:31](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/nc110-install-agentos.sh:31) correctly calls Linux even when breadcrumb saving returns failure. However, writes occur before arming, so a hung write is uncovered on cold boot. If `nc110_linux_now` returns, execution falls through into agent boot; its [Linux/initrd commands](~/nc110-baremetal/grub-test/nc110-linux-now.cfg:10) are unchecked. The fourth-boot branch clears the counter before successful Linux takeover. Stock recovery also lacks the custom route’s explicit finite watchdog-open timeout. Prove recovery-kernel/initramfs failures and inherited-watchdog takeover separately.

- **Decompression range calculation is incomplete.** [loader.rs:233](~/nc110-baremetal/src/loader.rs:233) uses `max(pref_address, dst)` without `relocatable_kernel` or alignment. The protocol requires aligned runtime placement for relocatable kernels and `pref_address` for nonrelocatable ones. Validate the actual interval, nonzero/sensible `init_size`, and its end below 4 GiB. The current local image happens to fit: preference/alignment both 16 MiB, initialization size `0x150b000`. [Linux boot protocol](https://docs.kernel.org/arch/x86/boot.html)

- **Header/map validation is permissive.** [loader.rs:251](~/nc110-baremetal/src/loader.rs:251): copying is memory-bounded, but accepts an undersized advertised header and silently caps larger headers at `0x290`. Require enough header for fields consumed; explicitly clear no-initrd/setup-data pointers after copying and respect `cmdline_size`. E820’s 20-byte layout and offsets are correct for valid GRUB records, but validate each record’s `size >= 20`, full stride, and complete consumption before accepting it.

The **entry mechanics look correct**: zeroed boot-parameter storage, terminated command line, E820 count/table offsets, CS `0x10`, data selectors `0x18`, paging/interrupts disabled, ESI pointing to parameters, and EBX/EDI/EBP zero. The leftover upper half of the pushed mode word is harmless. These match the [32-bit entry requirements](https://docs.kernel.org/arch/x86/boot.html#the-32-bit-boot-protocol).

**NIT:** Installer header still describes t2’s `next_entry=0` reboot; the runbook overstates “any hang” recovery.

**6/10**

# NC110 agent OS as the default boot (2026-10-18 22:43-23:00, evnchn's go 22:43, ask A179-1018-13)

- Entry `nc110-bm-agentos-t2-d4275899` (tools/nc110-install-agentos.sh): GRUB arms the TCO, then multiboot
  k-agentos-d4275899.elf with cmdline `bmctl` + module nc110-agentos.key (a dedicated key, ~/.nc110/agentos.key on the Mac,
  not in the repo; the test key is rejected). Any GRUB-side failure sets next_entry=0, saves nc110_fallback=<sha8> and reboots.
- grubenv: saved_entry=nc110-bm-agentos-t2-d4275899 (backup /boot/grub/grubenv.bak-agentos-*). Linux = manual fallback:
  hold Shift through POST, GRUB menu (30 s), "Alpine NC110, with Linux lts". Nothing on Slot A rewrites saved_entry
  (GRUB_SAVEDEFAULT=false; slotlab only reads grubenv).

| Test on the real netbook | Result |
|---|---|
| q35 GRUB 2.14 rehearsal of the exact entry bytes | boots, answers with the agent-OS key, test key rejected; missing kernel -> fallback branch |
| one-shot agent-OS boot from Linux | bmctl up, inherited TCO TMR=100 (GRUB-armed), key module verified |
| broken entry (kernel missing), default already agent OS | Linux for one boot, grubenv `nc110_fallback=fallbck0` (fallback proven) |
| plain reboot from Linux | **agent OS by default** in 20 s |
| network-loaded kernel that hangs (wdt=probe) | watchdog reset -> back in the **agent OS** in 138 s |
| bmctl `reboot` | back in the agent OS in 15 s |
| soak | tools/nc110-soak.sh -> proof/nc110-soak.log (started 23:00) |

## Round 2 (23:03-23:25): Codex 3/10 -> fixes, all tested on the real netbook
- **Remote route to Linux (finding 3):** the loader now boots an i386 Linux bzImage (32-bit boot protocol). The custom kernel
  6.18.44-agentnet is cross-built on the Mac. Agent OS -> `tools/nc110-to-linux.sh` -> **Slot A Linux with SSH, no hands**
  (9.3 MB staged in 127 s, SSH 10 s after the jump). q35 proof: `proof/linux-from-agentos-q35-serial.log`.
- **Crash-loop escape (finding 1) and same-boot fallback (finding 4):** template t3. The 4th agent-OS boot in one RTC hour
  goes to stock Linux. Proven: agent-OS boots 1-3, then **the 4th came up as 6.18.44-0-lts** with
  `nc110_fallback=loop-2026-10-18-23`. Every GRUB failure path ends in `nc110_linux_now`, with no grubenv write needed.
- Default now `nc110-bm-agentos-t3-aa8e0f26`, the kernel with the Linux route. Reboot from Linux goes back to the agent OS (verified).
- Open (documented): finding 2, a loaded kernel petting forever without bmctl, needs outside enforcement (a power switch);
  finding 6, a hang before GRUB arms.

## Round 3 (23:27-23:47): Codex 6/10 -> fixes, tested on the real netbook
- **Linux-visit lease (new MUST-FIX):** the pinger feeds the TCO only while `/dev/shm/nc110-lease` is younger than 10 min. Test: lease
  left unrenewed -> Linux answered SSH until t=630 s -> watchdog reset -> **t4 agent OS answered at t=651 s, no hands**.
- **Template t4:** a per-RTC-day boot budget (the hourly one missed slow loops) and a fail-closed counter (`^x{1,5}$`, else escape).
  Test: agent-OS boots 1-5, then **the 6th came up as stock 6.18.44-0-lts**, `nc110_fallback=loop-2026-10-18`. Reboot -> agent OS.
- **Loader:** decompression interval for relocatable/fixed kernels, header length, initrd/setup_data cleared, cmdline_size,
  strict E820 records. q35 Linux route and both suites re-run: PASS.
- Default: `nc110-bm-agentos-t4-f19a9a17`.
- **Still open (needs evnchn):** Codex finding 2. A loaded kernel that feeds the TCO but never serves bmctl can only be stopped from
  outside (a smart plug, and the battery out). Also open: a hang before GRUB arms the watchdog, and stock recovery Linux has no
  finite watchdog-open limit (a deliberate choice: it is the stable parking place).

codex
**Material improvement: findings 3 and 4 are fixed for the demonstrated path; finding 1 is substantially reduced.** Reviewed both commits and round-2 evidence; no edits or live fault injection.

| Original finding | Reassessment |
|---|---|
| **1. Persistent failures loop forever** | **Partially fixed / SHOULD-FIX.** Fast failures and default ~11-minute idle cycles now escape. Four boots within one RTC hour is not a general bounded-failure policy. |
| **2. Successor pets forever without bmctl** | **MUST-FIX, unchanged.** No independent recovery enforcement. |
| **3. No remote Linux maintenance route** | **Closed for the tested custom bzImage.** Hardware proof establishes agent OS → Slot A SSH. Loader hardening remains below. |
| **4. Failed `save_env` causes reboot loop** | **Closed for returned write errors with working Linux fallback.** Recovery no longer depends on successfully saving the breadcrumb. |
| **5. Idle reset overclaims channel health** | **SHOULD-FIX, unchanged.** Approximately 11 minutes; fresh authenticated requests refresh the deadline before reply delivery. Soak evidence improved, but does not demonstrate NIC fault recovery. |
| **6. Pre-GRUB-arm hangs** | **SHOULD-FIX, unchanged.** No established remote recovery before arming; successful normal resets do not cover this boundary. |

**MUST-FIX**

- **The Linux route introduces another indefinite petting case.** [nc110-to-linux.sh:16](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/nc110-to-linux.sh:16) starts a detached, unconditional watchdog pinger after one successful SSH connection. Subsequent NIC/SSH failure leaves Linux alive and petting forever. `open_timeout=300` protects the period before first open; it is not a continuing provisioning deadline. Require an expiring externally renewed lease or independent reset control.

**SHOULD-FIX**

- **Counter semantics need tightening.** [installer:20](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/nc110-install-agentos.sh:20): hour rollover discards accumulated failures. A repeatable failure producing a reboot every 21 minutes can run forever without four boots in any hour. Stable empty date variables produce `"---"` and still escape on boot four; intermittent valid/empty values or RTC jumps repeatedly clear the count. Exact equality with `"xxxx"` also fails open for malformed/overlong counter values. Prefer validated state and a failure budget cleared by confirmed provisioning health.

- **Fallback is an attempt, not a terminal recovery guarantee.** [installer:31](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/nc110-install-agentos.sh:31) correctly calls Linux even when breadcrumb saving returns failure. However, writes occur before arming, so a hung write is uncovered on cold boot. If `nc110_linux_now` returns, execution falls through into agent boot; its [Linux/initrd commands](~/nc110-baremetal/grub-test/nc110-linux-now.cfg:10) are unchecked. The fourth-boot branch clears the counter before successful Linux takeover. Stock recovery also lacks the custom route’s explicit finite watchdog-open timeout. Prove recovery-kernel/initramfs failures and inherited-watchdog takeover separately.

- **Decompression range calculation is incomplete.** [loader.rs:233](~/nc110-baremetal/src/loader.rs:233) uses `max(pref_address, dst)` without `relocatable_kernel` or alignment. The protocol requires aligned runtime placement for relocatable kernels and `pref_address` for nonrelocatable ones. Validate the actual interval, nonzero/sensible `init_size`, and its end below 4 GiB. The current local image happens to fit: preference/alignment both 16 MiB, initialization size `0x150b000`. [Linux boot protocol](https://docs.kernel.org/arch/x86/boot.html)

- **Header/map validation is permissive.** [loader.rs:251](~/nc110-baremetal/src/loader.rs:251): copying is memory-bounded, but accepts an undersized advertised header and silently caps larger headers at `0x290`. Require enough header for fields consumed; explicitly clear no-initrd/setup-data pointers after copying and respect `cmdline_size`. E820’s 20-byte layout and offsets are correct for valid GRUB records, but validate each record’s `size >= 20`, full stride, and complete consumption before accepting it.

The **entry mechanics look correct**: zeroed boot-parameter storage, terminated command line, E820 count/table offsets, CS `0x10`, data selectors `0x18`, paging/interrupts disabled, ESI pointing to parameters, and EBX/EDI/EBP zero. The leftover upper half of the pushed mode word is harmless. These match the [32-bit entry requirements](https://docs.kernel.org/arch/x86/boot.html#the-32-bit-boot-protocol).

**NIT:** Installer header still describes t2’s `next_entry=0` reboot; the runbook overstates “any hang” recovery.

**6/10**

**6/10**
   | NIC probe/init fails | Waits 60 seconds, resets into the same kernel |
   | DHCP fails | bmctl gives up after 15 seconds; subsequent reset retries the same kernel |
   | bmctl starts but never receives valid commands | Idle reset retries the same kernel |
   | Kernel hangs before its own arming | GRUB’s already-armed watchdog covers it, provided GRUB arming completed |
   | Kernel hangs after arming | Watchdog resets; persistent hang repeats |

   Transient failures can heal. A reproducible kernel/driver/key failure cannot be repaired remotely through the broken channel.

   **Needed:** a bounded boot-failure policy that reaches a known-good recovery environment automatically. Success must mean authenticated provisioning works, not merely that execution reached a petting loop. Preserve a recovery key/image independently of the shared agent key.

2. **A network-loaded kernel can pet forever without provisioning; nothing outside it enforces recovery.**  
   [loader.rs:201](~/nc110-baremetal/src/loader.rs:201)

   The loader checks image integrity, addresses and handoff safety. It cannot establish that the successor implements bmctl or honours the idle deadline. After jumping, the old supervisor is gone. A successor that pets indefinitely while networking is broken defeats the watchdog indefinitely.

   This is a concrete failure mode of privileged experimental kernels, not a hypothetical attacker requirement. **An independently enforced recovery deadline/reset path is needed** for the stated guarantee; another timer implemented inside the replacement kernel remains cooperative.

3. **There is no implemented remote route to Linux for routine persistent repairs.**  
   [bmctl.rs:436](~/nc110-baremetal/src/bmctl.rs:436), [loader.rs:1](~/nc110-baremetal/src/loader.rs:1)

   `reboot` returns to the agent OS. The loader changes RAM only and implements a constrained Multiboot handoff, not a demonstrated Linux boot path. Updating the `/boot` netloader, GRUB configuration, or on-disk key still requires Linux through the supplied installer.

   Shift is explicitly a hands-required route. Even with healthy bmctl, persistent maintenance lacks a supported remote transition. **Add and prove a remote recovery-boot operation**, plus an automatic escape when bmctl itself is unavailable.

4. **The GRUB error fallback depends on an unchecked write succeeding.**  
   [installer:36](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/nc110-install-agentos.sh:36)

   `save_env next_entry nc110_fallback` is followed unconditionally by `sleep` and `reboot`. If saving fails while the old environment remains readable, the next boot selects the failing agent entry again.

   Ext2 is not inherently a problem: GRUB supports its preallocated environment block on plain disks with non-checksumming filesystems through supported firmware disk access. But filesystem type alone does not guarantee writes succeed. The recorded missing-kernel test proves the happy write path only. [GNU GRUB environment-block documentation](https://www.gnu.org/software/grub/manual/grub/html_node/Environment-block.html)

   **Needed:** check persistence failure and provide a same-boot Linux fallback that does not require that write. Separately verify the deployed header’s `next_entry` consumption/clearing behaviour; that header is absent from the reviewed artifacts.

**SHOULD-FIX**

5. **The idle reset is useful, but weaker than “10-minute NIC self-heal.”**  
   [bmctl.rs:262](~/nc110-baremetal/src/bmctl.rs:262), [bmctl.rs:352](~/nc110-baremetal/src/bmctl.rs:352)

   Bounded receive polling and rejecting unauthenticated/replayed traffic as keepalives are good. A nonresponsive NIC normally causes the deadline to expire; a poll that hangs without further pets lets the hardware watchdog fire.

   However:
   - Expiry **returns** to `main`, which logs and pets for another 60 seconds before resetting. Normal recovery is roughly **11 minutes plus boot**, not ten.
   - Fresh authenticated requests refresh `last_ok` before successful reply delivery. A transmit-only failure can remain hidden if an existing client keeps sending fresh authenticated requests.
   - Reset recovery of an actual wedged NIC is not established by the provided proof. The soak log contains only its initial successful sample.

   Test receive-only/transmit-only failures and actual NIC reset recovery. Use an acknowledged round trip if the deadline is intended to represent channel health.

6. **Shift and pre-menu recovery remain unproven boundaries.**  
   [nc110-grubarm-func.cfg:1](~/nc110-baremetal/grub-test/nc110-grubarm-func.cfg:1), [proof:6](~/nc110-baremetal/proof/nc110-agentos-default.md:6)

   Arming occurs inside the selected entry. A cold-boot hang before that point has no protection established by these files. Conversely, if a running TCO survives a reboot, firmware must stop/reload it or reach recovery before expiry. A reset before GRUB reaches its keyboard check cannot be escaped by that check.

   I would **not claim such a loop currently exists**: the recorded successful watchdog reboot argues against it on the tested path. But the full deployed menu/header, Shift behaviour during repeated resets, and Linux’s takeover of an inherited watchdog need verification. Shift alone never satisfies remote recoverability.

**NIT**

- Comments still promise “Slot A on any reset” in `main.rs`, `loader.rs`, and older tooling. Those promises are now false and can mislead an operator during recovery.
- Reset loops are not automatically flash-wear loops: TCO register writes do not write `/boot`. GRUB fallback attempts do repeatedly write the environment block; Linux boots may write logs, and repeated resets impose hardware cycling. Quantitative wear cannot be inferred here. The immediate demonstrated risk is indefinite loss of provisioning.

**Score: 3/10 for “provisioning channel cannot break without needing hands.”**
     1	//! In-RAM chain loader, driven over bmctl (the DS OTA equivalent: nothing is written to /boot).
     2	//! `lbeg <size> <sha256>` opens a staging buffer at STAGE; binary `ldat` ++ u32 offset ++ bytes appends in order;
     3	//! `lcom <cmdline>` verifies length + SHA-256 + the multiboot header (a.out kludge, flag bit 16, as GRUB applies it),
     4	//! checks every destination range, builds a multiboot info block (cmdline, the ORIGINAL GRUB memory map, and a
     5	//! rebuilt 72-byte key module with this boot's seed), quiesces the NIC and jumps through the trampoline in boot.s,
     6	//! which turns paging off, copies the image to load_addr, zeroes its bss and enters it like GRUB would.
     7	//! `lcomlinux <cmdline>` instead boots the staged image as an i386 Linux bzImage (32-bit boot protocol, no initrd):
     8	//! the route from the agent OS back to Slot A Linux without hands (Codex agent-OS review finding 3).
     9	//! The TCO stays armed across the hop (the attended successor re-arms it in kmain). Any refusal leaves us running.
    10	use crate::kprintln;
    11	use crate::mb;
    12	use crate::nic::Rtl;
    13	use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
    14	
    15	pub const STAGE: u64 = 0x0400_0000; // 64 MiB
    16	pub const CAP: u64 = 0x0200_0000; // 32 MiB (bzImage-sized; STAGE + CAP = 96 MiB < 128 MiB, so q35 -m 128 works)
    17	const TRAMP: u64 = 0x8000; // trampoline code + its stack (< 0xC00)
    18	const PARAMS: u64 = 0x8C00;
    19	const INFO: u64 = 0x8D00;
    20	const MODENT: u64 = 0x8E80;
    21	const CMDLINE: u64 = 0x8F00; // <= 255 bytes + NUL
    22	const MODULE: u64 = 0x9000; // page-aligned (our header asks for it: flag bit 0)
    23	const MMAP: u64 = 0x9100;
    24	const LOW_END: u64 = 0xA000;
    25	const MMAP_MAX: usize = (LOW_END - MMAP) as usize;
    26	
    27	/// GRUB's boot metadata, copied out at boot (before any staging could overwrite it). (flags, mem_lower, mem_upper)
    28	static mut ORIG: (u32, u32, u32) = (0, 0, 0);
    29	static mut ORIG_MMAP: [u8; MMAP_MAX] = [0; MMAP_MAX];
    30	static mut ORIG_MMAP_LEN: usize = 0;
    31	static mut ORIG_MMAP_TOO_BIG: bool = false;
    32	
    33	/// Call once, early, with the multiboot info pointer GRUB (or a previous loader) gave us.
    34	pub fn snapshot_boot_info(info: u64) {
    35	    if info == 0 {
    36	        return;
    37	    }
    38	    unsafe {
    39	        let r32 = |a: u64| core::ptr::read_volatile(a as *const u32);
    40	        let f = r32(info);
    41	        ORIG = (f, if f & 1 != 0 { r32(info + 4) } else { 0 }, if f & 1 != 0 { r32(info + 8) } else { 0 });
    42	        if f & (1 << 6) != 0 {
    43	            let (l, a) = (r32(info + 44) as usize, r32(info + 48) as u64);
    44	            if l <= MMAP_MAX {
    45	                core::ptr::copy(a as *const u8, core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(ORIG_MMAP) as *mut u8, l);
    46	                ORIG_MMAP_LEN = l;
    47	            } else {
    48	                ORIG_MMAP_TOO_BIG = true;
    49	            }
    50	        }
    51	    }
    52	}
    53	
    54	extern "C" {
    55	    static tramp_start: u8;
    56	    static tramp_end: u8;
    57	    static __load_start: u8;
    58	    static __bss_end: u8;
    59	}
    60	
    61	pub struct Stage {
    62	    size: u64,
    63	    sha: [u8; 32],
    64	    got: u64,
    65	}
    66	
    67	fn overlaps(a: u64, al: u64, b: u64, bl: u64) -> bool {
    68	    a < b.saturating_add(bl) && b < a.saturating_add(al)
    69	}
    70	
    71	fn kernel_image() -> (u64, u64) {
    72	    let (s, e) = (core::ptr::addr_of!(__load_start) as u64, core::ptr::addr_of!(__bss_end) as u64);
    73	    (s, e - s)
    74	}
    75	
    76	pub fn begin(size: u64, sha_hex: &str) -> Result<Stage, &'static str> {
    77	    if size == 0 || size > CAP {
    78	        return Err("size 1..32 MiB");
    79	    }
    80	    let (ks, kl) = kernel_image();
    81	    if !mb::ram_contains(STAGE, size) || overlaps(STAGE, size, ks, kl) {
    82	        return Err("staging area not free RAM");
    83	    }
    84	    if sha_hex.len() != 64 || !sha_hex.bytes().all(|c| c.is_ascii_hexdigit()) {
    85	        return Err("sha256: 64 hex chars");
    86	    }
    87	    let mut sha = [0u8; 32];
    88	    for (i, b) in sha.iter_mut().enumerate() {
    89	        *b = u8::from_str_radix(&sha_hex[2 * i..2 * i + 2], 16).map_err(|_| "sha256: hex")?;
    90	    }
    91	    Ok(Stage { size, sha, got: 0 })
    92	}
    93	
    94	pub fn data(st: &mut Stage, off: u64, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<u64, &'static str> {
    95	    if off != st.got {
    96	        return Err("out of order (send chunks in sequence)");
    97	    }
    98	    let end = off.checked_add(bytes.len() as u64).ok_or("overflow")?;
    99	    if end > st.size {
   100	        return Err("past announced size");
   200	    Ok(Plan { params: [src, dst, len, bss, entry, INFO, 0] })
   201	}
   202	
   203	const BP: u64 = 0x10000; // Linux boot_params (zero page)
   204	const BP_CMD: u64 = 0x11000; // Linux command line
   205	
   206	/// Linux 32-bit boot protocol (Documentation/arch/x86/boot.rst): protected-mode code goes to code32_start, a zero
   207	/// page carries the setup header, the command line and an E820 map built from GRUB's multiboot map.
   208	pub fn prepare_linux(st: &Stage, cmdline: &str) -> Result<Plan, &'static str> {
   209	    if st.got != st.size {
   210	        return Err("incomplete image");
   211	    }
   212	    let img = unsafe { core::slice::from_raw_parts(STAGE as *const u8, st.size as usize) };
   213	    let d: [u8; 32] = Sha256::digest(img).into();
   214	    if d != st.sha {
   215	        return Err("sha256 mismatch");
   216	    }
   217	    if img.len() < 0x1000 || img[0x1fe..0x200] != [0x55, 0xAA] || &img[0x202..0x206] != b"HdrS" {
   218	        return Err("not a bzImage (boot flag / HdrS)");
   219	    }
   220	    let r16 = |o: usize| u16::from_le_bytes([img[o], img[o + 1]]) as u64;
   221	    let r32 = |o: usize| u32::from_le_bytes(img[o..o + 4].try_into().unwrap()) as u64;
   222	    let ver = r16(0x206);
   223	    if ver < 0x020a || img[0x211] & 1 == 0 {
   224	        return Err("bzImage boot protocol < 2.10 or not LOADED_HIGH");
   225	    }
   226	    let setup = if img[0x1f1] == 0 { 4 } else { img[0x1f1] as u64 };
   227	    let off = (setup + 1) * 512;
   228	    // the header must cover every field read below (init_size ends at 0x264)
   229	    if 0x202 + img[0x201] as usize <= 0x264 {
   230	        return Err("setup header too short for the fields this loader uses");
   231	    }
   232	    let (dst, init_size, pref) = (r32(0x214), r32(0x260), r32(0x258) | r32(0x25c) << 32);
   233	    let (kalign, reloc, cmax) = (r32(0x230), img[0x234] != 0, r32(0x238));
   234	    let len = st.size.checked_sub(off).ok_or("image shorter than its setup")?;
   235	    if dst != 0x10_0000 || dst + len > STAGE || !mb::ram_contains(dst, len) {
   236	        return Err("protected-mode code must load at 1 MiB, below staging, in RAM");
   237	    }
   238	    // Where the kernel decompresses and runs (boot.rst): pref_address if not relocatable; if relocatable, the load
   239	    // address aligned up to kernel_alignment, or pref_address. Both candidate intervals must be RAM below 4 GiB.
   240	    if init_size == 0 || (reloc && !kalign.is_power_of_two()) {
   241	        return Err("bad init_size / kernel_alignment");
   242	    }
   243	    let aligned = if reloc { (dst + kalign - 1) & !(kalign - 1) } else { pref };
   244	    for base in [pref, aligned] {
   245	        match base.checked_add(init_size) {
   246	            Some(end) if end <= 0x1_0000_0000 && mb::ram_contains(base, init_size) => {}
   247	            _ => return Err("kernel's runtime range (init_size at pref/aligned address) not RAM below 4 GiB"),
   248	        }
   249	    }
   250	    let run = pref;
   251	    if cmdline.len() > 2047 || cmdline.len() as u64 > cmax || !mb::ram_contains(BP, 0x2000) {
   252	        return Err("cmdline longer than 2047 / the kernel's cmdline_size, or boot_params pages not RAM");
   253	    }
   254	    let tl = (core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_end) as u64) - (core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_start) as u64);
   255	    if tl > PARAMS - TRAMP || !mb::ram_contains(TRAMP, LOW_END - TRAMP) {
   256	        return Err("low pages 0x8000-0xA000 not usable");
   257	    }
   258	    unsafe {
   259	        if ORIG_MMAP_TOO_BIG || ORIG_MMAP_LEN == 0 {
   260	            return Err("no usable GRUB memory map snapshot for the E820 table");
   261	        }
   262	        core::ptr::write_bytes(BP as *mut u8, 0, 0x2000);
   263	        core::ptr::write_bytes(TRAMP as *mut u8, 0, (LOW_END - TRAMP) as usize);
   264	        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_start), TRAMP as *mut u8, tl as usize);
   265	        let hend = (0x202 + img[0x201] as usize).min(0x290);
   266	        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(img.as_ptr().add(0x1f1), (BP + 0x1f1) as *mut u8, hend - 0x1f1);
   267	        for o in [0x218u64, 0x21c, 0x250, 0x254] {
   268	            core::ptr::write_volatile((BP + o) as *mut u32, 0); // no initrd, no setup_data chain
   269	        }
   270	        core::ptr::write_volatile((BP + 0x210) as *mut u8, 0xFF); // type_of_loader: undefined
   271	        core::ptr::write_volatile((BP + 0x228) as *mut u32, BP_CMD as u32); // cmd_line_ptr
   272	        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(cmdline.as_ptr(), BP_CMD as *mut u8, cmdline.len());
   273	        // E820 from the multiboot map: entries {size u32, base u64, len u64, type u32}, stride size + 4
   274	        let mm = &*core::ptr::addr_of!(ORIG_MMAP);
   275	        let (mut p, mut n) = (0usize, 0u64);
   276	        while p < ORIG_MMAP_LEN {
   277	            let sz = u32::from_le_bytes(mm[p..p + 4].try_into().unwrap()) as usize;
   278	            if sz < 20 || p + 4 + sz > ORIG_MMAP_LEN || n >= 128 {
   279	                return Err("GRUB memory map record malformed (size < 20, overrun) or > 128 records");
   280	            }
   281	            let e = BP + 0x2d0 + 20 * n;
   282	            core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(mm.as_ptr().add(p + 4), e as *mut u8, 20);
   283	            n += 1;
   284	            p += sz + 4;
   285	        }
   286	        core::ptr::write_volatile((BP + 0x1e8) as *mut u8, n as u8);
   287	    }
   288	    kprintln!("loader: Linux ready: bzImage proto {:#x}, {} B pm code -> {:#x}, init_size {:#x} at {:#x}, cmdline '{}'", ver, len, dst, init_size, run, cmdline);
   289	    Ok(Plan { params: [STAGE + off, dst, len, 0, dst, BP, 1] })
   290	}
   291	
   292	/// Point of no return: NIC quiesced (reset completed), trampoline entered. The TCO stays armed across the hop.
   293	/// If the NIC reset does not complete, its DMA may still target memory the new image would overwrite: hard reset
   294	/// instead of jumping (GRUB default, the agent OS, then the crash-loop escape).
   295	pub fn jump(p: &Plan, nic: &Rtl) -> ! {
   296	    if let Err(e) = nic.quiesce() {
   297	        kprintln!("loader: NIC quiesce failed ({}): NOT jumping, hard reset", e);
   298	        crate::wdt::hard_reset();
   299	    }
   300	    unsafe {
   301	        for (i, v) in p.params.iter().enumerate() {
   302	            core::ptr::write_volatile((PARAMS + 8 * i as u64) as *mut u64, *v);
   303	        }
   304	        crate::wdt::pet();
   305	        let f: extern "sysv64" fn(u64) -> ! = core::mem::transmute(TRAMP as usize);
   306	        f(PARAMS)
   307	    }
   308	}

    nc110_linux_now

  fi

    true

    echo "nc110-agentos f19a9a17: grubenv not writable, Slot A Linux now"

      if module /baremetal/nc110-agentos.key; then

    fi
    echo "nc110-agentos f19a9a17: kernel or key module failed to load"
  else
    echo "nc110-agentos f19a9a17: GRUB did NOT arm the TCO"
  fi
  set nc110_fallback=f19a9a17
  save_env nc110_fallback
  nc110_linux_now
}

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# From the agent OS to Slot A Linux with no hands (Codex agent-OS finding 3): chain-load the custom i386 kernel
# (~/nc110-kbuild, SLOTB-BUILD-FORMULA config + PACKET=y, no initrd, no modules) with root=/dev/sda2.
# watchdog.open_timeout=300: the kernel feeds the inherited TCO for at most 5 min unless something opens
# /dev/watchdog, so a Linux that never comes up resets back into the agent OS (the GRUB default) by itself.
# Once SSH answers, a LEASE pinger holds /dev/watchdog: it feeds the TCO only while /dev/shm/nc110-lease is younger than
# 10 min, so a Linux that loses its agent (NIC/SSH dies) resets back into the agent OS within ~11 min (Codex MUST-FIX).
# Renew from the Mac:  ssh root@192.168.50.118 touch /dev/shm/nc110-lease    `reboot` returns to the agent OS at once.
set -u
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
BZ=${BZ:-$HOME/nc110-kbuild/linux-6.18.44/arch/x86/boot/bzImage}
S() { ssh -F /dev/null -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=5 -o StrictHostKeyChecking=no -o UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null \
  -o LogLevel=ERROR -i "$HOME/.ssh/id_ed25519_homelab" root@192.168.50.118 "$@"; }
tools/.venv/bin/python tools/bmctl.py linux "$BZ" root=/dev/sda2 ro rootfstype=ext4 rootwait console=tty0 watchdog.open_timeout=300 || exit 1
T0=$(date +%s)
for _ in $(seq 60); do
  sleep 5
  if S 'uname -r; hostname' 2>/dev/null; then
    S "touch /dev/shm/nc110-lease; setsid sh -c 'exec 3>/dev/watchdog; while [ \$((\$(date +%s) - \$(stat -c %Y /dev/shm/nc110-lease))) -lt 600 ]; do printf 1 >&3; sleep 10; done' </dev/null >/dev/null 2>&1 &" \
      && echo "Slot A Linux up after $(( $(date +%s) - T0 )) s; lease pinger holds /dev/watchdog (renew: touch /dev/shm/nc110-lease every < 10 min)."
    exit 0
  fi
done
echo "Linux did not answer SSH within 5 min; the 300 s watchdog open_timeout returns the board to the agent OS"; exit 3

/// Record available-RAM ranges (mmap type 1, or mem_lower/mem_upper as a fallback).
pub fn parse_memory(info: u64) {
    unsafe {
        if info == 0 {
            return;
        }
        let flags = rd32(info);
        if flags & (1 << 6) != 0 {
            let len = rd32(info + 44) as u64;
            let mut p = rd32(info + 48) as u64;
            let end = p + len;
            while p + 24 <= end && NRAM < 32 {
                let size = rd32(p) as u64;
                let base = read_volatile((p + 4) as *const u64);
                let l = read_volatile((p + 12) as *const u64);
                let ty = rd32(p + 20);
                if ty == 1 && l > 0 {
                    RAM[NRAM] = (base, l);
                    NRAM += 1;
                }
                p += size + 4;
            }
            HAVE_MAP = true;
        } else if flags & 1 != 0 {
            RAM[0] = (0, rd32(info + 4) as u64 * 1024);
            RAM[1] = (0x10_0000, rd32(info + 8) as u64 * 1024);
            NRAM = 2;
            HAVE_MAP = true;
        }
    }
}

/// True if [base, base+len) touches available RAM, or if no memory map was given (fail closed).
pub fn ram_overlaps(base: u64, len: u64) -> bool {
    unsafe {
        if !HAVE_MAP {
            return true;
        }
        let ram = &*core::ptr::addr_of!(RAM);
        ram[..NRAM].iter().any(|&(b, l)| base < b + l && b < base + len)
    }
}

/// True only if [base, base+len) lies entirely inside ONE available-RAM range of the multiboot map.
pub fn ram_contains(base: u64, len: u64) -> bool {
    let Some(end) = base.checked_add(len) else { return false };
    unsafe {
        let ram = &*core::ptr::addr_of!(RAM);
        HAVE_MAP && ram[..NRAM].iter().any(|&(b, l)| base >= b && end <= b.saturating_add(l))
    }
}

**Improved, but still short of unattended recovery assurance.** Reviewed `37c2cd1`, rounds 2–3, and the printed `t4-f19a9a17` entry. Shell syntax checks passed; no edits or live fault injection.

| Finding | Reassessment |
|---|---|
| **1. Persistent boot loops** | **SHOULD-FIX, substantially improved.** Sixth-boot escape is hardware-proven. Daily rollover still erases failures; alternating valid/empty dates also resets the budget. Use a persistent failure budget cleared by confirmed channel health. **Software-fixable.** |
| **2. Third-party kernel pets forever** | **MUST-FIX, unchanged.** Once privileged successor code owns the machine, this supervisor cannot enforce recovery. Needs **independent external reset/power control**; an AC smart plug requires battery removal and verified unattended power-on. |
| **3. Remote Linux maintenance route** | **Closed for the demonstrated custom kernel.** Round 2 proves agent OS → Linux SSH. Loader caveats below remain software-fixable. |
| **4. `save_env` failure loop** | **Closed for returned errors with working Linux fallback.** Same-boot Linux does not depend on persistence succeeding. A hung write before watchdog arming remains uncovered. |
| **5. Idle reset versus channel health** | **SHOULD-FIX, unchanged.** Fresh authenticated requests refresh `last_ok` before successful reply delivery. Require evidence of bidirectional progress; NIC-fault recovery remains unproven. **Software-fixable.** |
| **6. Pre-arm hangs** | **SHOULD-FIX, unchanged.** Earlier arming can cover more GRUB operations; BIOS/pre-GRUB hangs require **external control**. |
| **Linux-visit lease MUST-FIX** | **Closed for the tested ordinary-expiry case:** reset and agent response at **651 s**. **SHOULD-FIX:** wall-clock subtraction accepts negative ages, so backward clock changes extend the lease. Use a monotonic deadline. |

**Remaining concrete issues:**

- **MUST-FIX — stock recovery Linux still lacks a provisioning deadline.** The sixth-boot escape happens before this entry arms TCO, and stock Linux has neither the custom route’s finite open timeout nor its lease setup. Landing there successfully does not guarantee recovery from later SSH/NIC failure. Calling it a stable parking place does not satisfy the stated guarantee. **Software-fixable:** watchdog coverage plus an externally renewed lease on this route too. [Recovery entry](~/nc110-baremetal/grub-test/nc110-linux-now.cfg:10)

- **SHOULD-FIX — t4 fallthrough is documented, not fully hardened.** The printed regexp correctly permits boots 1–5 and rejects the sixth/malformed same-day counter. Linux returning now explicitly proceeds toward agent boot. However, `linux`/`initrd` remain unchecked: an initrd failure can reach `boot`, preventing that useful fallthrough. The counter is also cleared before successful recovery takeover. [Installer](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/nc110-install-agentos.sh:17)

- **SHOULD-FIX — decompression interval remains slightly wrong.** Relocatable runtime placement is `align_up(max(dst, pref), alignment)`. Checking `pref` and `align_up(dst, alignment)` separately misses the actual interval when `pref` is unaligned. Fixed-kernel placement, nonzero size and below-4-GiB checks improved. The tested aligned image is unaffected. [Loader](~/nc110-baremetal/src/loader.rs:243), [protocol](https://docs.kernel.org/arch/x86/boot.html)

- **SHOULD-FIX — E820 validation reads before checking remaining length.** Require four bytes remaining before `mm[p..p+4]`; a truncated final size field can read beyond the recorded map or panic at the backing-array boundary. Record-size, stride and count checks otherwise improved. Initrd/setup-data clearing and command-line limits look correct. [Loader](~/nc110-baremetal/src/loader.rs:276)

- **NIT — header boundary:** exclusive end `0x264` already covers `init_size`; reject `< 0x264`, not `<=`. [Loader](~/nc110-baremetal/src/loader.rs:229)

**“Provisioning channel cannot break without needing hands”: 7/10.**  
**Excluding finding 2 (third-party kernels): 8/10.**

| **5. Idle reset versus channel health** | **SHOULD-FIX, unchanged.** Fresh authenticated requests refresh `last_ok` before successful reply delivery. Require evidence of bidirectional progress; NIC-fault recovery remains unproven. **Software-fixable.** |
| **6. Pre-arm hangs** | **SHOULD-FIX, unchanged.** Earlier arming can cover more GRUB operations; BIOS/pre-GRUB hangs require **external control**. |
| **Linux-visit lease MUST-FIX** | **Closed for the tested ordinary-expiry case:** reset and agent response at **651 s**. **SHOULD-FIX:** wall-clock subtraction accepts negative ages, so backward clock changes extend the lease. Use a monotonic deadline. |

**Remaining concrete issues:**

- **MUST-FIX — stock recovery Linux still lacks a provisioning deadline.** The sixth-boot escape happens before this entry arms TCO, and stock Linux has neither the custom route’s finite open timeout nor its lease setup. Landing there successfully does not guarantee recovery from later SSH/NIC failure. Calling it a stable parking place does not satisfy the stated guarantee. **Software-fixable:** watchdog coverage plus an externally renewed lease on this route too. [Recovery entry](~/nc110-baremetal/grub-test/nc110-linux-now.cfg:10)

- **SHOULD-FIX — t4 fallthrough is documented, not fully hardened.** The printed regexp correctly permits boots 1–5 and rejects the sixth/malformed same-day counter. Linux returning now explicitly proceeds toward agent boot. However, `linux`/`initrd` remain unchecked: an initrd failure can reach `boot`, preventing that useful fallthrough. The counter is also cleared before successful recovery takeover. [Installer](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/nc110-install-agentos.sh:17)

- **SHOULD-FIX — decompression interval remains slightly wrong.** Relocatable runtime placement is `align_up(max(dst, pref), alignment)`. Checking `pref` and `align_up(dst, alignment)` separately misses the actual interval when `pref` is unaligned. Fixed-kernel placement, nonzero size and below-4-GiB checks improved. The tested aligned image is unaffected. [Loader](~/nc110-baremetal/src/loader.rs:243), [protocol](https://docs.kernel.org/arch/x86/boot.html)

- **SHOULD-FIX — E820 validation reads before checking remaining length.** Require four bytes remaining before `mm[p..p+4]`; a truncated final size field can read beyond the recorded map or panic at the backing-array boundary. Record-size, stride and count checks otherwise improved. Initrd/setup-data clearing and command-line limits look correct. [Loader](~/nc110-baremetal/src/loader.rs:276)

- **NIT — header boundary:** exclusive end `0x264` already covers `init_size`; reject `< 0x264`, not `<=`. [Loader](~/nc110-baremetal/src/loader.rs:229)

**“Provisioning channel cannot break without needing hands”: 7/10.**  
**Excluding finding 2 (third-party kernels): 8/10.**

+# Appended once to the NC110 grub.cfg (2026-10-18, template t5). Recovery boot of stock Slot A Linux in the SAME boot,
+# now WITH a deadline (Codex MUST-FIX): GRUB arms the TCO first (best effort), the cmdline carries nc110.lease=600
+# (Slot A's /etc/local.d/nc110-lease.start then needs an agent to renew, else clean reboot into the agent OS) and
+# watchdog.open_timeout=300 (if userspace never opens /dev/watchdog, the kernel stops feeding the TCO after 5 min).
+function nc110_linux_now2 {
+  echo "nc110: booting Slot A Linux now (recovery, 10-min agent lease)"
+  nc110_tco_consts
+  tco_arm
+  insmod gzio
+  insmod part_msdos
+  insmod ext2
+  search --no-floppy --fs-uuid --set=root c0954bc7-86a0-440c-8401-2c3e95549743
+  if linux /vmlinuz-lts root=UUID=b86fb3d7-5127-4800-b36b-b47279ee86ae ro quiet modules=sd-mod,ahci,ata_piix,ext4 rootfstype=ext4 rootdelay=5 nc110.lease=600 watchdog.open_timeout=300; then
+    if initrd /initramfs-lts; then
+      boot
+    fi
+  fi
+  echo "nc110: Slot A Linux failed to load"
+}

diff --git a/tools/slot-a/nc110-lease.start b/tools/slot-a/nc110-lease.start
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..05d3051
--- /dev/null
+++ b/tools/slot-a/nc110-lease.start
@@ -0,0 +1,26 @@
+#!/bin/sh
+# /etc/local.d/nc110-lease.start on Slot A (installed by the NC110 pane, 2026-10-18). Active ONLY when the kernel
+# cmdline carries nc110.lease=<s> (agent routes: the agent OS's Linux hop and GRUB's crash-loop recovery); a manual
+# boot from the Shift menu is untouched. It holds /dev/watchdog and feeds it while an agent keeps renewing
+# /dev/shm/nc110-lease (touch it); renewal is detected by mtime CHANGE and aged on /proc/uptime, so wall-clock jumps
+# cannot extend it. On expiry: sync + clean reboot (GRUB default = the agent OS); if this loop itself dies, the TCO
+# resets the board. Remove this file to disable.
+L=$(sed -n 's/.*nc110\.lease=\([0-9]*\).*/\1/p' /proc/cmdline)
+[ -n "$L" ] || exit 0
+F=/dev/shm/nc110-lease
+touch "$F"
+setsid sh -c '
+  up() { cut -d. -f1 /proc/uptime; }
+  exec 3>/dev/watchdog || exit 0
+  last_m=$(stat -c %Y '"$F"'); last_u=$(up)
+  while :; do
+    m=$(stat -c %Y '"$F"' 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
+    [ "$m" != "$last_m" ] && { last_m=$m; last_u=$(up); }
+    if [ $(( $(up) - last_u )) -ge '"$L"' ]; then
+      logger -t nc110-lease "lease expired after '"$L"' s without renewal: sync + reboot into the agent OS"
+      sync; reboot; sleep 120
+    fi
+    printf 1 >&3
+    sleep 10
+  done' </dev/null >/dev/null 2>&1 &
+exit 0

exec
