   206	/// Linux 32-bit boot protocol (Documentation/arch/x86/boot.rst): protected-mode code goes to code32_start, a zero
   207	/// page carries the setup header, the command line and an E820 map built from GRUB's multiboot map.
   208	pub fn prepare_linux(st: &Stage, cmdline: &str) -> Result<Plan, &'static str> {
   209	    if st.got != st.size {
   210	        return Err("incomplete image");
   211	    }
   212	    let img = unsafe { core::slice::from_raw_parts(STAGE as *const u8, st.size as usize) };
   213	    let d: [u8; 32] = Sha256::digest(img).into();
   214	    if d != st.sha {
   215	        return Err("sha256 mismatch");
   216	    }
   217	    if img.len() < 0x1000 || img[0x1fe..0x200] != [0x55, 0xAA] || &img[0x202..0x206] != b"HdrS" {
   218	        return Err("not a bzImage (boot flag / HdrS)");
   219	    }
   220	    let r16 = |o: usize| u16::from_le_bytes([img[o], img[o + 1]]) as u64;
   221	    let r32 = |o: usize| u32::from_le_bytes(img[o..o + 4].try_into().unwrap()) as u64;
   222	    let ver = r16(0x206);
   223	    if ver < 0x020a || img[0x211] & 1 == 0 {
   224	        return Err("bzImage boot protocol < 2.10 or not LOADED_HIGH");
   225	    }
   226	    let setup = if img[0x1f1] == 0 { 4 } else { img[0x1f1] as u64 };
   227	    let off = (setup + 1) * 512;
   228	    // the header must cover every field read below (init_size ends at 0x264)
   229	    if (0x202 + img[0x201] as usize) < 0x264 {
   230	        return Err("setup header too short for the fields this loader uses");
   231	    }
   232	    let (dst, init_size, pref) = (r32(0x214), r32(0x260), r32(0x258) | r32(0x25c) << 32);
   233	    let (kalign, reloc, cmax) = (r32(0x230), img[0x234] != 0, r32(0x238));
   234	    let len = st.size.checked_sub(off).ok_or("image shorter than its setup")?;
   235	    if dst != 0x10_0000 || dst + len > STAGE || !mb::ram_contains(dst, len) {
   236	        return Err("protected-mode code must load at 1 MiB, below staging, in RAM");
   237	    }
   238	    // Where the kernel decompresses and runs (boot.rst): pref_address if not relocatable; if relocatable, the load
   239	    // address aligned up to kernel_alignment, or pref_address. Both candidate intervals must be RAM below 4 GiB.
   240	    if init_size == 0 || (reloc && !kalign.is_power_of_two()) {
   241	        return Err("bad init_size / kernel_alignment");
   242	    }
   243	    // relocatable: align_up(max(load address, pref_address), kernel_alignment); fixed: pref_address
   244	    let runat = if reloc { (dst.max(pref) + kalign - 1) & !(kalign - 1) } else { pref };
   245	    for base in [runat] {
   246	        match base.checked_add(init_size) {
   247	            Some(end) if end <= 0x1_0000_0000 && mb::ram_contains(base, init_size) => {}
   248	            _ => return Err("kernel's runtime range (init_size at pref/aligned address) not RAM below 4 GiB"),
   249	        }
   250	    }
   251	    let run = runat;
   252	    if cmdline.len() > 2047 || cmdline.len() as u64 > cmax || !mb::ram_contains(BP, 0x2000) {
   253	        return Err("cmdline longer than 2047 / the kernel's cmdline_size, or boot_params pages not RAM");
   254	    }
   255	    let tl = (core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_end) as u64) - (core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_start) as u64);
   256	    if tl > PARAMS - TRAMP || !mb::ram_contains(TRAMP, LOW_END - TRAMP) {
   257	        return Err("low pages 0x8000-0xA000 not usable");
   258	    }
   259	    unsafe {
   260	        if ORIG_MMAP_TOO_BIG || ORIG_MMAP_LEN == 0 {
   261	            return Err("no usable GRUB memory map snapshot for the E820 table");
   262	        }
   263	        core::ptr::write_bytes(BP as *mut u8, 0, 0x2000);
   264	        core::ptr::write_bytes(TRAMP as *mut u8, 0, (LOW_END - TRAMP) as usize);
   265	        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(core::ptr::addr_of!(tramp_start), TRAMP as *mut u8, tl as usize);
   266	        let hend = (0x202 + img[0x201] as usize).min(0x290);
   267	        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(img.as_ptr().add(0x1f1), (BP + 0x1f1) as *mut u8, hend - 0x1f1);
   268	        for o in [0x218u64, 0x21c, 0x250, 0x254] {
   269	            core::ptr::write_volatile((BP + o) as *mut u32, 0); // no initrd, no setup_data chain
   270	        }
   271	        core::ptr::write_volatile((BP + 0x210) as *mut u8, 0xFF); // type_of_loader: undefined
   272	        core::ptr::write_volatile((BP + 0x228) as *mut u32, BP_CMD as u32); // cmd_line_ptr
   273	        core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(cmdline.as_ptr(), BP_CMD as *mut u8, cmdline.len());
   274	        // E820 from the multiboot map: entries {size u32, base u64, len u64, type u32}, stride size + 4
   275	        let mm = &*core::ptr::addr_of!(ORIG_MMAP);
   276	        let (mut p, mut n) = (0usize, 0u64);
   277	        while p < ORIG_MMAP_LEN {
   278	            if p + 4 > ORIG_MMAP_LEN {
   279	                return Err("GRUB memory map ends inside a record header");
   280	            }
   281	            let sz = u32::from_le_bytes(mm[p..p + 4].try_into().unwrap()) as usize;
   282	            if sz < 20 || p + 4 + sz > ORIG_MMAP_LEN || n >= 128 {
   283	                return Err("GRUB memory map record malformed (size < 20, overrun) or > 128 records");
   284	            }
   285	            let e = BP + 0x2d0 + 20 * n;
   286	            core::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(mm.as_ptr().add(p + 4), e as *mut u8, 20);
   287	            n += 1;
   288	            p += sz + 4;
   289	        }
   290	        core::ptr::write_volatile((BP + 0x1e8) as *mut u8, n as u8);
   291	    }
   292	    kprintln!("loader: Linux ready: bzImage proto {:#x}, {} B pm code -> {:#x}, init_size {:#x} at {:#x}, cmdline '{}'", ver, len, dst, init_size, run, cmdline);
   293	    Ok(Plan { params: [STAGE + off, dst, len, 0, dst, BP, 1] })
   294	}
   295	
   296	/// Point of no return: NIC quiesced (reset completed), trampoline entered. The TCO stays armed across the hop.
   297	/// If the NIC reset does not complete, its DMA may still target memory the new image would overwrite: hard reset
   298	/// instead of jumping (GRUB default, the agent OS, then the crash-loop escape).
   299	pub fn jump(p: &Plan, nic: &Rtl) -> ! {
   300	    if let Err(e) = nic.quiesce() {
   301	        kprintln!("loader: NIC quiesce failed ({}): NOT jumping, hard reset", e);
   302	        crate::wdt::hard_reset();
   303	    }
   304	    unsafe {
   305	        for (i, v) in p.params.iter().enumerate() {
   306	            core::ptr::write_volatile((PARAMS + 8 * i as u64) as *mut u64, *v);
   307	        }
   308	        crate::wdt::pet();
   309	        let f: extern "sysv64" fn(u64) -> ! = core::mem::transmute(TRAMP as usize);
   310	        f(PARAMS)
   311	    }
   312	}

static int watchdog_release(struct inode *inode, struct file *file)
{
	struct watchdog_core_data *wd_data = file->private_data;
	struct watchdog_device *wdd;
	int err = -EBUSY;
	bool running;

	mutex_lock(&wd_data->lock);

	wdd = wd_data->wdd;
	if (!wdd)
		goto done;

	/*
	 * We only stop the watchdog if we received the magic character
	 * or if WDIOF_MAGICCLOSE is not set. If nowayout was set then
	 * watchdog_stop will fail.
	 */
	if (!watchdog_active(wdd))
		err = 0;
	else if (test_and_clear_bit(_WDOG_ALLOW_RELEASE, &wd_data->status) ||
		 !(wdd->info->options & WDIOF_MAGICCLOSE))
		err = watchdog_stop(wdd);

	/* If the watchdog was not stopped, send a keepalive ping */
	if (err < 0) {
		pr_crit("watchdog%d: watchdog did not stop!\n", wdd->id);
		watchdog_ping(wdd);
	}

	watchdog_update_worker(wdd);

	/* make sure that /dev/watchdog can be re-opened */
	clear_bit(_WDOG_DEV_OPEN, &wd_data->status);

done:
	running = wdd && watchdog_hw_running(wdd);
	mutex_unlock(&wd_data->lock);
	/*
	 * Allow the owner module to be unloaded again unless the watchdog
	 * is still running. If the watchdog is still running, it can not
	 * be stopped, and its driver must not be unloaded.
	 */
	if (!running) {
		break;
	case 3:
		outl(0x20008, TCO1_STS(p));
		break;
	case 2:
	case 1:
	default:
		outw(0x0008, TCO1_STS(p)); /* Clear the Time Out Status bit */
		outw(0x0002, TCO2_STS(p)); /* Clear SECOND_TO_STS bit */
		outw(0x0004, TCO2_STS(p)); /* Clear BOOT_STS bit */
		break;
	}

	ident.firmware_version = p->iTCO_version;
	p->wddev.info = &ident;
	p->wddev.ops = &iTCO_wdt_ops;
	p->wddev.bootstatus = 0;
	p->wddev.timeout = WATCHDOG_TIMEOUT;
	watchdog_set_nowayout(&p->wddev, nowayout);
	p->wddev.parent = dev;

	watchdog_set_drvdata(&p->wddev, p);
	platform_set_drvdata(pdev, p);

	if (!iTCO_wdt_set_running(p)) {
		/*
		 * If the watchdog was not running set NO_REBOOT now to
		 * prevent later reboots.
		 */
		p->update_no_reboot_bit(p->no_reboot_priv, true);
	}

	/* Check that the heartbeat value is within it's range;
	   if not reset to the default */
	if (iTCO_wdt_set_timeout(&p->wddev, heartbeat)) {
		ret = iTCO_wdt_set_timeout(&p->wddev, WATCHDOG_TIMEOUT);
		if (ret != 0) {
			dev_err(dev, "Failed to set watchdog timeout (%d)\n", WATCHDOG_TIMEOUT);
			return ret;
		}
		dev_info(dev, "timeout value out of range, using %d\n",
			WATCHDOG_TIMEOUT);
		heartbeat = WATCHDOG_TIMEOUT;
	}

	watchdog_stop_on_reboot(&p->wddev);
	watchdog_stop_on_unregister(&p->wddev);
	ret = devm_watchdog_register_device(dev, &p->wddev);
	if (ret != 0)
		return ret;

	dev_info(dev, "initialized. heartbeat=%d sec (nowayout=%d)\n",
		heartbeat, nowayout);

	return 0;
}

menuentry "nc110-bm-agentos-t5-49b98b1e" {
  insmod ext2
  search --no-floppy --fs-uuid --set=root c0954bc7-86a0-440c-8401-2c3e95549743
  insmod datehook
  insmod regexp
  set nc110_now="${YEAR}-${MONTH}-${DAY}"
  if [ "$nc110_hour" != "$nc110_now" ]; then
    set nc110_hour="$nc110_now"
    set nc110_boots=""
  fi
  set nc110_boots="${nc110_boots}x"
  if regexp '^x{1,5}$' "$nc110_boots"; then
    true
  else
    echo "nc110-agentos 49b98b1e: 6th agent-OS boot today (or a malformed counter), escaping to Slot A Linux"
    set nc110_boots=""
    set nc110_fallback="loop-$nc110_now"
    save_env nc110_hour nc110_boots nc110_fallback
    nc110_linux_now2
    echo "nc110-agentos 49b98b1e: Slot A Linux failed to load, trying the agent OS anyway"
  fi
  if save_env nc110_hour nc110_boots; then
    true
  else
    echo "nc110-agentos 49b98b1e: grubenv not writable, Slot A Linux now"
    nc110_linux_now2
  fi
  terminal_output console
  set gfxpayload=text
  insmod multiboot
  nc110_tco_consts
  tco_arm
  if [ "$wdt_ok" = "yes" ]; then
    echo "nc110-agentos 49b98b1e: TCO armed by GRUB, loading /baremetal/k-agentos-49b98b1e.elf (boots today: $nc110_boots)"
    if multiboot /baremetal/k-agentos-49b98b1e.elf bmctl; then
      if module /baremetal/nc110-agentos.key; then
        boot
      fi
    fi
    echo "nc110-agentos 49b98b1e: kernel or key module failed to load"
  else
    echo "nc110-agentos 49b98b1e: GRUB did NOT arm the TCO"
  fi
  set nc110_fallback=49b98b1e
  save_env nc110_fallback
  nc110_linux_now2
}

#else
static inline bool __maybe_unused need_suspend(void) { return true; }
#endif

static int __maybe_unused iTCO_wdt_suspend_noirq(struct device *dev)
{
	struct iTCO_wdt_private *p = dev_get_drvdata(dev);
	int ret = 0;

	p->suspended = false;
	if (watchdog_active(&p->wddev) && need_suspend()) {
		ret = iTCO_wdt_stop(&p->wddev);
		if (!ret)
			p->suspended = true;
	}
	return ret;
}

codex
**The missing-deadline MUST-FIX is closed for normal recovery boots:** t5 routes fallbacks through the leased Linux entry; round 4 records agent OS recovery at **629 s**. It does **not** yet prove recovery from every lease/reboot failure.

The decompression interval, E820 remaining-header check, header boundary, and checked `linux`/`initrd` fallthrough are fixed. Lease aging now uses uptime correctly; wall-clock changes alone don’t renew an unchanged file.

**Remaining software-fixable items:**

- **MUST-FIX — shutdown can remove watchdog protection.** iTCO registers `watchdog_stop_on_reboot`; the reboot notifier directly stops it, bypassing the ordinary `nowayout` check. A subsequent shutdown hang can therefore need hands. Preserve watchdog coverage through restart, or use watchdog expiry as the terminal recovery action. [Driver](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.18/drivers/watchdog/iTCO_wdt.c), [local notifier](~/nc110-kbuild/linux-6.18.44/drivers/watchdog/watchdog_core.c:159).
- **SHOULD-FIX — expiry must be terminal.** In the [lease script](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/slot-a/nc110-lease.start:19), hung `logger`, `sync`, or userspace `reboot` stops feeding, so an armed TCO should recover. But `sleep 120` subsequently falls through to another feed if reset hasn’t happened. Never resume feeding after expiry.
- **SHOULD-FIX — service robustness/deployment.** The shell constructs are BusyBox-ash compatible; `%Y` provides second-resolution renewal detection, and `cut` extracts uptime seconds correctly. Validate failed reads instead of treating stat failure as a changed timestamp; check watchdog opening/writes and service readiness. The tracked script is **0644**, and the installer doesn’t deploy it—make executable installation and OpenRC activation reproducible.
- **SHOULD-FIX — legacy route remains.** [to-linux](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/nc110-to-linux.sh:19) retains the wall-clock fallback pinger; its `pgrep -f` readiness check can match the checking shell. Replace it with verified service/device ownership.
- Earlier issues remain: date-reset boot budget, pre-arm GRUB operations, and authenticated incoming traffic counting as health without demonstrated reply delivery.
- **Minor loader hardening:** use checked alignment arithmetic; `pref + alignment - 1` can overflow before the subsequent checked range test.

**Needs hardware/external control:**

- Finding 2 remains: privileged successor kernels can feed forever without provisioning.
- BIOS/pre-GRUB hangs need independent reset/power control.
- **Hardware validation still needed:** kill/stop the lease loop and inject stalled shutdown/NIC failures. Ordinary loop death should leave TCO running because the script never writes magic-close `V`; round 4 tested clean reboot, not this path. [Watchdog release behavior](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.18/drivers/watchdog/watchdog_dev.c).

**Scores: 7/10 overall; 8/10 excluding finding 2.** Improvements are real, but the shutdown protection gap prevents an increase. Read-only review and shell syntax checks; no live fault injection or edits.
